\displaystyle\left\|g-\psi_{l}(g)\right\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l}\lambda.

Step 1.

Observe that by union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\left(\lambda\right)}D_{nm}\left(g\right)\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\left(\lambda\right)}D_{nm}\left(\psi_{0}\left(g\right)\right)\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(g)-D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\bigr).

(25)

Since
D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
\left\|\psi_{0}(g)\right\|_{nm}\sigma_{\epsilon}\leq\lambda\sigma_{\epsilon}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{\psi_{0}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{0}}D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\psi_{0}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\left\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\lambda^{-1})\right\}-\frac{c_{0}t^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{c_{0}t^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}\right),

(26)

where the last inequality follows if
t\geq\lambda\sqrt{2C^{\prime}\phi_{nm}nm\left\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\lambda^{-1})\right\}}.

Step 2.

Note that

\displaystyle D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))-D_{nm}(g)=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}D_{nm}(\psi_{l-1}(g))-D_{nm}(\psi_{l}(g)).

Therefore, for
\eta_{l}\geq 0
and
\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\eta_{l}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(g)-D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\exists l\in Z^{+},\text{such that}\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(\psi_{l}(g))-D_{nm}(\psi_{l-1}(g))\geq\eta_{l}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\bigcup_{l=1}^{\infty}\bigl\{\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(\psi_{l}(g))-D_{nm}(\psi_{l-1}(g))\geq\eta_{l}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(\psi_{l}(g))-D_{nm}(\psi_{l-1}(g))\geq\eta_{l}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{\psi_{l-1}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{l-1},\psi_{l}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{l}}D_{nm}(\psi_{l}(g))-D_{nm}(\psi_{l-1}(g))\geq\eta_{l}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\psi_{l-1}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{l-1},\psi_{l}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{l}}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(D_{nm}(\psi_{l-1}(g)-\psi_{l}(g))\geq\eta_{l}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\bigr).

Since

\displaystyle\|\psi_{l-1}(g)-\psi_{l}(g)\|_{nm}\leq\|\psi_{l-1}(g)-g\|_{nm}+\|\psi_{l}(g)-g\|_{nm}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2^{-l+1}\lambda+2^{-l}\lambda=3\cdot 2^{-l}\lambda\leq 2^{-l+2}\lambda,

and

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathcal{T}_{l-1}\bigr|\leq\bigl|\mathcal{T}_{l}\bigr|\leq\exp\bigl(C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)\bigl\{l\log(2)+\log\bigl(\lambda^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}\bigr),

by plugging
\|\psi_{l-1}(g)-\psi_{l}(g)\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+2}\lambda
in (
23
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(g)-D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\psi_{l-1}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{l-1},\psi_{l}(g)\in\mathcal{T}_{l}}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(D_{nm}(\psi_{l-1}(g)-\psi_{l}(g))\geq\eta_{l}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}2\exp\bigl(C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)\bigl\{l\log(2)+\log\bigl(\lambda^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}\bigr)\exp\bigl(-\frac{c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}\eta_{l}^{2}t^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigr),

for some
c^{\prime\prime}>0
.

If
C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)\left\{l\log(2)+\log(\lambda^{-1})\right\}\leq\frac{c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}\eta_{l}^{2}t^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}
, or simply

\displaystyle\eta_{l}^{2}\geq\frac{2C^{\prime}\lambda^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)\left\{l\log(2)+\log(\lambda^{-1})\right\}}{c^{\prime\prime}t^{2}2^{2l}},

(27)

following the previous derivation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(g)-D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}2\exp\bigl(C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)\bigl\{l\log(2)+\log\bigl(\lambda^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}\bigr)\exp\bigl(-\frac{c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}\eta_{l}^{2}t^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}2\exp\left(-\frac{c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}\eta_{l}^{2}t^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}\right).

Since
t\geq\lambda\sqrt{2C^{\prime}\phi_{nm}nm\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\lambda^{-1})}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2C^{\prime}\lambda^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)\left\{l\log(2)+\log(\lambda^{-1})\right\}}{c^{\prime\prime}t^{2}2^{2l}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}}+\frac{l\log(2)}{\log(\lambda^{-1})c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}}=\frac{l}{c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}}\left(\frac{1}{l}+\frac{\log(2)}{\log(\lambda^{-1})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}l}{c^{\prime\prime}2^{2l}},

for some constant
\frac{1}{2}>C_{1}>0
, where the last inequality holds by
\lambda\leq\frac{1}{4}
.

Thus, to satisfy (
27
),
it suffices to choose

\eta_{l}^{2}=C_{1}l2^{-2l}
for some positive constant
C_{1}
. Furthermore, with such
\eta_{l}
, we have
\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\eta_{l}=\sqrt{C_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}l^{1/2}2^{-l}.
For large
l
,
2^{-l}
decreases exponentially faster than
l^{1/2}
increases, so the series
\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}l^{1/2}2^{-l}
converges. And further by
\frac{1}{2}>C_{1}
, we have
\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\eta_{l}\leq 1
. Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}D_{nm}(g)-D_{nm}(\psi_{0}(g))\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}2\exp\left(-\frac{c^{\prime\prime}lt^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}\right)\leq\exp(-\frac{c^{\prime\prime\prime}t^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}).

(28)

for some positive constant
c^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
By (
25
), (
26
), (
28
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\left(\lambda\right)}D_{nm}(g)\geq\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{c^{\prime}t^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\right),

for some positive constant
c^{\prime}
.
∎

Corollary 6
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a function class such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{nm})\leq C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\bigl(\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1})\bigr)

(29)

and that
\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\infty}\left(\tau\right)
, where
\tau\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Then it holds that for any
s\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{D_{mn}\bigl(g\bigr)}{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)).

Proof.

Since

\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\infty}\left(\tau\right)\subset B_{nm}\left(\tau\right)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\left(\tau\right)=\mathcal{F}.

Therefore, it suffices to show that for any
s\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl(\tau\bigr)}\frac{D_{mn}\bigl(g\bigr)}{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log\bigl(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)).

Choose
\lambda=2^{-l+1}\tau
in
Corollary

5
. For a positive constant
C_{1}^{\prime}
that depends on
C^{\prime}
, if
s\geq 1
and

\displaystyle t\geq C_{1}^{\prime}s2^{-l+1}\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log\bigl((2^{-l+1}\tau)^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}},

it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(2^{-l+1}\tau)}D_{nm}(g)\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s2^{-l+1}\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr\}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\left\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigl(-c^{\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\log(2)\bigr):=\exp\bigl(-c^{\prime\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\bigr),

for some positive constants
c^{\prime\prime}
and
c^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
where the second inequality holds since
\tau\leq\frac{1}{2}
, so
\tau^{-1}2^{l-1}\geq 2^{l}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\left\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\right\}}D_{nm}(g)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s2^{-l+1}\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl(\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\right).

Note that if

g\in\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}
, then
\|g\|^{-1}_{nm}\geq\tau^{-1}2^{l-1}
and
2^{-l+1}\tau\leq 2\left\|g\right\|_{nm}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}D_{nm}(g)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s2^{-l+1}\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr\}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{D_{mn}\bigl(g\bigr)}{2^{-l+1}\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{D_{mn}\bigl(g\bigr)}{2\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|^{-1}_{nm})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{D_{mn}\bigl(g\bigr)}{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|^{-1}_{nm})\bigr\}}}\geq 2C_{1}^{\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\bigr).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{D_{mn}\bigl(g\bigr)}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log\bigl(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl(\tau\bigr)}\frac{D_{nm}(g)}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\bigr)

\displaystyle=\ \mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\Bigg(\exists l\in Z^{+},\text{such that}

\displaystyle\,\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\bigl\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\bigr\}}\frac{D_{nm}(g)}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{D_{nm}(g)}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}

\displaystyle\quad\left.\geq C_{1}\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot s\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)),

where the first identity follows from a peeling argument, noticing that
\bigcup_{l=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}=\mathcal{F}\cap\bigcup_{l=1}^{\infty}\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}=\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\tau)
.
∎

S3.1.2
Deviation bounds for
\xi

Suppose
\{\xi_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables and denote

\displaystyle H(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{nm_{i}}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}.

In
Corollary

7
,
Corollary

8
and
Corollary

9
, the analysis is conditioning on
\{x_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
. We denote the conditional probability conditioning on
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
by
\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}
.

Corollary 7
.

Let
g,f\in{\mathcal{L}_{2}}
be two deterministic functions such that
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq\tau,\|f\|_{\infty}\leq\tau
. Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(H\left(g\right)\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{\tau^{2}\left\|g\right\|_{mn}^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(H\left(g\right)-H\left(f\right)\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{\tau^{2}\|f-g\|_{mn}^{2}}\right).

Proof.

For the first bound, it suffices to note that

\left\{g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
’s are independent sub-Gaussian with parameter
g^{2}(x_{ij})
. So
H(g)
is sub-Gaussian with parameter

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{4}(x_{ij})}\leq\tau\left\|g\right\|_{mn},

since
\left\|g\right\|_{\infty}\leq\tau
.
For the second bound, it suffices to note that

\left\{\left(f^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij})\right)\xi_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
are independent sub-Gaussian with parameter
\left\{|f^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij})|\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
. So
H(f)-H(g)
is sub-Gaussian with parameter

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}(f^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij}))^{2}}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}(f(x_{ij})-g(x_{ij}))^{2}(f(x_{ij})+g(x_{ij}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{4\tau^{2}}{nm_{i}}(f(x_{ij})-g(x_{ij}))^{2}}\leq 2\tau\|f-g\|_{mn}.

Since
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq\tau,\|f\|_{\infty}\leq\tau
, the claim follows.
∎

Corollary 8
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a function class such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{nm})\leq C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\bigl(\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1})\bigr),

and suppose that
g\in\mathcal{F}
implies that
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq\tau
.
For

\displaystyle t\geq C^{\prime\prime}\lambda\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\left\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\lambda^{-1})\right\}}

with
\tau\leq\frac{1}{2}
,
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}H_{nm}(g)\geq t\right)\leq\exp(-\frac{c^{\prime}t^{2}}{\tau^{2}\lambda^{2}}),

for some positive constants
c^{\prime}
and
C^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof of the above bound follows from the same argument as in
Corollary

5
.
∎

Corollary 9
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a function class such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{nm})\leq C^{\prime}nm\phi_{nm}\bigl(\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1})\bigr),

and suppose that
g\in\mathcal{F}
implies that
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq\tau
, where
\tau\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
For any
s\geq 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\tau s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)).

Proof.

Since

\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\infty}\left(\tau\right)\subset B_{nm}\left(\tau\right)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\left(\tau\right)=\mathcal{F}.

Therefore it suffices to show that for any
s\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl(\tau\bigr)}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log\bigl(\tau\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\tau s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\exp\left(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}\left(n\right)\right).

Choose
\lambda=2^{-l+1}\tau
in
Corollary

8
. For any
s\geq 1
, and

\displaystyle t\geq C_{1}^{\prime}s2^{-l+1}\tau^{2}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log\bigl((2^{-l+1}\tau)^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\}}

it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(2^{-l+1}\tau)}H_{nm}(g)\geq C_{1}^{\prime}s2^{-l+1}\tau^{2}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\tau^{-1}\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr\}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\left\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(2^{l-1}\tau^{-1})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\left\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(2^{l})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\log\left(2\right)\right),

for a positive constant
c^{\prime\prime}
.
Thus, for a positive constant
c^{\prime\prime\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}H_{nm}(g)

\displaystyle\quad\geq C_{1}^{\prime}s2^{-l+1}\tau^{2}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr\}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\right).

Note that if

g\in\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}
, then
\|g\|^{-1}_{nm}\geq\tau^{-1}2^{l-1}
and
2^{-l+1}\tau\leq 2\left\|g\right\|_{nm}
.It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau^{2}\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}H_{nm}(g)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\geq C_{1}^{\prime}s2^{-l+1}\tau^{2}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr\}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau^{2}\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{2^{-l+1}\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\tau^{-1}2^{l-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\tau s\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau^{2}\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{2\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}^{\prime}\tau s\bigr).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\tau s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\tau)}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\tau s\bigr)

\displaystyle=\ \mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\Bigg(\exists l\in Z^{+},\text{such that}

\displaystyle\,\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\bigl\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\bigr\}}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\tau s\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}}\frac{H_{nm}(g)}{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\tau s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-c^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}s^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)l\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)),

where the first identity follows from a peeling argument that
\bigcup_{l=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}\cap\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}=\mathcal{F}\cap\bigcup_{l=1}^{\infty}\{f:2^{-l}\tau\leq\|f\|_{nm}\leq 2^{-l+1}\tau\}=\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\tau)
.
∎

For
Corollary

10
, we assume
x
is a vector of independent random variables. The general case is given in Section
S3.2.3
,
Lemma

20
.

Corollary 10
.

Let
n
be sufficiently large. For any
\eta\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
, there exists a constant
C_{\eta}
only depending on
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)\leq 12.

Consequently, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}_{\eta}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\big|\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}|}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C^{\prime}_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\right)\geq 1-24\eta.

(30)

Proof.

By the symmetrization argument,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})-\mathbb{E}(g^{2}(x_{ij}))}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x^{\prime}\mid x}(g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x^{\prime}_{ij}))}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{x^{\prime}\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}(g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x^{\prime}_{ij}))}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}(g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x^{\prime}_{ij}))}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\xi_{ij}(g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x^{\prime}_{ij}))}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\xi_{ij}g^{2}(x_{ij})}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)+\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\xi_{ij}g^{2}(x^{\prime}_{ij})}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\xi_{ij}g^{2}(x_{ij})}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right),

where we use
{\mathbb{E}}_{x^{\prime}\mid x}
to indicate
{\mathbb{E}}_{\{x^{\prime}_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}\mid\{x_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}}
,
where
\left\{x^{\prime}_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
are ghost samples of
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
, which means that
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}
and
\left\{x^{\prime}_{ij}\right\}
are i.i.d drawn from the same distribution as
\left\{x^{\prime}_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
and
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
are independent of
\left\{x^{\prime}_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
. In addition, we use
x_{ij}
to denote a random variable that follows the same distribution as
x_{ij}
.
\left\{\xi_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
are i.i.d. Rademacher variables. The fourth identity follows from the fact that
\xi_{ij}(g(x_{ij})-g(x^{\prime}_{ij}))
and
g(x_{ij})-g(x^{\prime}_{ij})
have the same distribution.

We use
{\mathbb{E}}_{x}
to indicate
{\mathbb{E}}_{\{x_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}}
, and use
{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}
to indicate
{\mathbb{E}}_{\{\xi_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}\mid\{x_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}}
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=2{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle=2{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\cdot\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\cdot\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\bigl[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)\bigr].

Step 1.

Denote

\displaystyle B_{nm}(a,b)=\{g:a\leq\|g\|_{nm}\leq b\}.

Observe that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1}\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1},\infty)}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr).

Since
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}\leq\|g\|_{nm}^{2}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1})}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1})}\bigl|\frac{\frac{1}{(nm)^{2}}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{\eta}(nm)^{2}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}.

In addition,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1},\infty\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1},\infty)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\bigr|\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}1ds+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\bigr|\geq s\bigr)ds,

where the first inequality follows from the fact that
g\in B_{nm}((nm)^{-1},\infty)
so that
\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\leq\log(nm)
.

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\bigr|\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}}\bigr|\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}}\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}}\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{2}\int_{1}^{\infty}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm))ds

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{cnm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-u^{2})du=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}C_{2}}{\sqrt{cnm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)}},

where the third inequality follows from
Corollary

9
for sufficiently large positive constant
C_{\eta}
and the fact that
\{-\xi_{ij}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables.
So for sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1}\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)+

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1},\infty\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{\eta}(nm)^{2}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}+1+\frac{\sqrt{\pi}C_{2}}{\sqrt{cnm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)}}\leq\frac{3}{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}-\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{x}\bigl[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\mathbb{E}_{\xi\mid x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g^{2}\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)\xi_{ij}}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5}(nm)}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5}(nm)}\cdot\frac{3}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right).

Step 2.

Note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}-1\right)+1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\left|\eta\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}-\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\right|}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right)+1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}-\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)+1.

(31)

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5}(nm)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\eta{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}-\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)+3,

i.e.

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}-\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)\leq\frac{3}{1-3\eta}

for any
0\leq\eta<1/3
.
Since
0\leq\eta<1/4
, this implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}-\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)\leq 12.

Step 3.

Let
C_{\eta}^{\prime}:=C_{\eta}/\eta
, so that
\eta C_{\eta}^{\prime}=C_{\eta}
. Then, for every
g\in\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)=\frac{1}{\eta}\Big(\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)\Big).

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\bigl|\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr|}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}=\eta\,\frac{\bigl|\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr|}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}.

Taking the supremum over
g\in\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|=\eta\,\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\geq\frac{1}{2\eta}\right).

Since the random variable inside the probability is nonnegative, by Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\geq\frac{1}{2\eta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\,\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right).

Using the expectation bound established previously,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)\leq 12,

we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}\right)\leq 24\eta.

Equivalently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\big|\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\big|}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\right)\geq 1-24\eta.

∎

S3.1.3
Deviation bounds for
\gamma

Conditioning on
\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
,
we aim to bound

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\lambda)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\left\{g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\left\langle g,\gamma_{i}\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\right\}.

Suppose
\left\{\xi_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables and denote

\displaystyle J_{nm}(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{nm_{i}}}g\left(x_{ij}\right)\gamma_{i}\left(x_{ij}\right)\xi_{ij}.

In
Corollary

11
and
Corollary

12
, the analysis is conditioning on
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
, as well as conditioning on
\left\{\gamma_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
as mentioned above.
We denote the conditional probability conditioning on
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
by
\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}}
, and conditioning on
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
and
\left\{\gamma_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
by
\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}},\left\{\gamma_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}}
, and we use
\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}
as their shorthand notations.

Corollary 11
.

Let
g,f\in{\mathcal{L}_{2}}
be two deterministic functions,
furthermore, let
\|\gamma\|_{\infty,nm}\leq\tau
. Here
\left\|\gamma_{i}\right\|_{\infty,nm}:=\max_{1\leq j\leq m_{i}}\left|\gamma_{i}\left(x_{ij}\right)\right|.
Then conditioning on
\{x_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
, and
\left\{\gamma_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\left(J_{nm}(g)\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{\tau^{2}\|g\|_{nm}^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\left(J_{nm}(g)-J_{n}(f)\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{\tau^{2}\|f-g\|_{nm}^{2}}\right).

Proof.

For the first bound, it suffices to note that

g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
|g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})|\
. So
J(g)
is sub-Gaussian with parameter

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}^{2}(x_{ij})}\leq\tau\|g\|_{nm}.

The second bound follows from the fact that
J(g)
is linear in
g
. The second part of this lemma follows by the same argument.
∎

Corollary 12
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a function class such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}\left(\delta,\mathcal{F},\left\|\cdot\right\|_{nm}\right)\leq nm\phi_{nm}\left(\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1})\right)

and that
\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\infty}(\tau)
.
For

\displaystyle t\geq C\lambda\tau\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\left\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\lambda^{-1})\right\}},

it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{n}(\lambda)}J_{nm}(g)\geq t\right)\leq\exp(-\frac{c^{\prime}t^{2}}{\tau^{2}\lambda^{2}}).

In addition, for any
s\geq 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{J_{nm}(g)}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{nm\phi_{nm}\bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\bigr\}}}\geq C_{1}\tau s\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)).

Proof.

The proof of the first bound follows from the same argument as in
Corollary

8
and the second bound follows from the same argument as in
Corollary

9
.
∎

Corollary 13
.

Let
n
be sufficiently large and suppose that
\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\infty,nm}:=\max_{1\leq j\leq m_{i}}\left|\gamma_{i}\left(x_{ij}\right)\right|\leq\tau
,
there exists a constant
C_{\eta}
only depending on
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl[g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle g,\gamma_{i}\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\bigr]}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)\leq 12.

Consequently, for any
\eta\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}_{\eta}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl[g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle g,\gamma_{i}\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\bigr]}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\leq\frac{1}{16}\bigr)\geq 1-192\eta.

(32)

Proof.

By symmetrization argument,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\left[g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle g,\gamma_{i}\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\right]}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle=2{\mathbb{E}}_{x,\gamma}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle=2{\mathbb{E}}_{x,\gamma}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\cdot\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{x,\gamma}\bigl[{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{x,\gamma}\bigl[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)\bigr].

Step 1.

Denote

\displaystyle B_{nm}(a,b)=\{g:a\leq\|g\|_{nm}\leq b\}.

Observe that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1})}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)+

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1},\infty)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr).

Since

\displaystyle\bigl|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}\bigr|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl|g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij})}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\gamma_{i}^{2}(x_{ij})}\leq\tau\|g\|_{nm},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1}\bigr)}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1})}\big|\frac{\tau\|g\|_{nm}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1})}\bigl|\frac{\frac{\tau}{nm}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}((nm)^{-1})}\big|\frac{\frac{\tau}{nm}}{\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)\leq\frac{1}{C_{\eta}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}.

In addition,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1},\infty\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1},\infty\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\big|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\big|\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}1ds+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\big|\geq s\bigr)ds.

where the first inequality follows from the restriction on the function class that
\|g\|_{nm}\geq\frac{1}{nm}
, which implies that
\|g\|^{-1}_{nm}\leq nm
.

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}\bigr|\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}}\big|\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}}\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\|g\|_{nm}\sqrt{\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{nm}^{-1})\}}}\geq s\bigr)ds

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{2}\int_{1}^{\infty}\exp(-cs^{2}nm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm))ds

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{cnm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-u^{2})du=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}C_{2}}{\sqrt{cnm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)}},

where the second inequality follows from
Corollary

12
for sufficiently large constant
C_{\eta}
and the fact that
\{-\xi_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
are also i.i.d. Rademacher random variables.
Since
r=o\left({nm\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right)
, for sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1}\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)\gamma_{i}\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)+

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}\bigl((nm)^{-1},\infty\bigr)}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)\gamma_{i}\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau^{2}}{C_{\eta}(nm)^{2}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}+1+\frac{\sqrt{\pi}C_{2}}{\sqrt{cnm\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm)}}\leq\frac{3}{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{x,\gamma}\bigl[\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi|x,\gamma}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)\gamma_{i}\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 3{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right).

Step 2.

Note that since
0\leq\eta\leq 1/4

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\eta\|g\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\eta{\mathbb{E}}_{x}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\bigr)+1\leq 12\eta+1=4.

where the first inequality follows from (
31
), and the second inequality follows from
Corollary

10
.
Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\left[g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle g,\gamma_{i}\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\right]}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\xi_{ij}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\big|\right)\leq 12.

Step 3.

By Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl[g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle g,\gamma_{i}\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\bigr]}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\geq\frac{1}{16}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl[g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle g,\gamma_{i}\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\bigr]}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau\eta C_{\eta}^{\prime}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\geq\frac{1}{16\eta}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 16\eta\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl[g(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle g,\gamma_{i}\rangle_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}\bigr]}{\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)}\bigr|\bigr)\leq 192\eta,

where
\eta C_{\eta}^{\prime}=C_{\eta}
.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Suppose
X
is a sub-Gaussian random variable and that for some constant
c
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\exp(cX^{2}))<\infty.

For any
t>0
, let

\mathcal{E}=\{|X|\leq t\}
and suppose that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1/2
. Then

X|\mathcal{E}
is also a sub-Gaussian random variable.

Proof.

For any
A\subset\mathbb{R}
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X\in A|\mathcal{E})=\frac{\mathbb{P}(X\in A\text{ and }|X|\leq t)}{\mathbb{P}(|X|\leq t)}\leq 2\mathbb{P}(X\in A).

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\exp(cX^{2})|\mathcal{E})=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\exp(cX^{2})\geq s|\mathcal{E})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}2\mathbb{P}(\exp(cX^{2})\geq s)ds=2\mathbb{E}(\exp(cX^{2}))<\infty.

∎

S3.2
Deviation Bounds for Temporal Dependence with
\beta
-mixing Conditions

Now we address the general case of
\epsilon
and
x
. The proof strategy involves constructing ’ghost’ versions of
\epsilon
and
x
that are independent but closely mimic the behavior of the original
\epsilon
and
x
. The details are provided below.

S3.2.1
The
\beta
-mixing Lemmas for Theorem 1

We begin by recalling the notation for sigma-fields and
\beta
-mixing that will be used throughout this subsection.

Let
X
be a random variable taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We denote by
\sigma(X)
the sigma-field generated by
X
. More generally, for a collection of random variables
\{X_{t}:t\in I\}
, where
I\subseteq\mathbf{Z}^{+}
, we write

\displaystyle\sigma(X_{t},\ t\in I)

for the sigma-field they generate.

For two sigma-fields
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, define the
\beta
-mixing coefficient by

\displaystyle\beta(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{C\in\mathcal{B}}\big|\mathbb{P}(C)-\mathbb{P}(C\mid\mathcal{A})\big|\Big].

A sequence of sigma-fields
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}}
is said to be
\beta
-mixing if

\displaystyle\beta_{s}=\sup_{t\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}}\beta\!\bigl(\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{\ell},\,\ell\leq t),\,\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{\ell},\,\ell\geq t+s)\bigr)\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }s\to\infty.

Accordingly, a sequence of random variables
\{X_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}}
is called
\beta
-mixing if the associated sequence of sigma-fields
\{\sigma(X_{t})\}_{t\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}}
is
\beta
-mixing.

A important Theorem we will use is present below.

Theorem 15

(
(
Doukhan, 2012
, Theorem 1, Section 1.2.1)
)
.

Let
E
and
F
be two Polish spaces, and let
(X,Y)
be an
E\times F
-valued random element defined on a probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
. Set

\displaystyle\beta=\beta(\sigma(X),\sigma(Y)).

Assume that the probability space is rich enough to support a random variable

\displaystyle\Delta\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]

that is independent of
\sigma(X,Y)
.

Then there exists an
F
-valued random element
Y^{*}
such that:

1.

Y^{*}
has the same distribution as
Y
;

2.

Y^{*}
is independent of
X
;

3.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Y\neq Y^{*})=\beta.

Moreover, there exists a measurable function

\displaystyle f:E\times F\times[0,1]\to F

such that

\displaystyle Y^{*}=f(X,Y,\Delta).

Throughout this subsection, for any Polish space
E
, we write
\mathcal{B}(E)
for its Borel sigma-field. In particular,
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
denotes the Borel sigma-field on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Although Theorem
15
only requires the existence of a single auxiliary random variable
\Delta
independent of
\sigma(X,Y)
, our proof applies this coupling argument repeatedly at different indices. To ensure that each application uses fresh independent randomness, we enlarge the probability space, if necessary, so that it carries an i.i.d. sequence

\displaystyle\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}

of
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables that are independent of the original process.

One convenient construction is to work on the product space

\displaystyle\bigl(\widetilde{\Omega},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)=\bigl(\Omega\times[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}},\ \mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{B}([0,1]^{\mathbb{N}}),\ \mathbb{P}\otimes\lambda^{\otimes\mathbb{N}}\bigr),

where
\lambda
denotes Lebesgue measure on
[0,1]
, and define

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}(\omega,u_{1},u_{2},\ldots)=u_{i}.

Then
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
are i.i.d.
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
and are independent of the sigma-field generated by the original coordinate, namely

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\otimes\{\emptyset,[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}}\}.

For integers
a,b
and
m\geq 1
, we write

\displaystyle a\equiv b\pmod{m}

to mean that
m
is a divisor
a-b
.

For any
S\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and

\displaystyle K=\lfloor n/S\rfloor,

define

\mathcal{I}_{k}=\begin{cases}((k-1)S,\ kS]&\text{if }1\leq k\leq K,\\[3.00003pt]
(KS,\ n]&\text{if }k=K+1.\end{cases}

For
0\leq s\leq S-1
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{e,s}=\{\,i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}:k\text{ is even and }i\equiv s\ (\mathrm{mod}\ S)\,\},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{o,s}=\{\,i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}:k\text{ is odd and }i\equiv s\ (\mathrm{mod}\ S)\,\}.

Then

\displaystyle\frac{K-1}{2}\leq|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|\leq\frac{K+2}{2},\qquad\frac{K-1}{2}\leq|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|\leq\frac{K+2}{2},

(33)

and

\displaystyle\bigcup_{s=0}^{S-1}\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\ \cup\ \bigcup_{s=0}^{S-1}\mathcal{J}_{o,s}=\{1,\ldots,n\}.

We first apply the coupling theorem to the covariate process. The next lemma shows that, on each subcollection
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
or
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
, one can construct coupled covariate blocks that preserve the marginal distribution of the original blocks, are jointly independent, and differ from the originals only with probability at most
\beta(S)
.

Lemma 16
.

Let

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}(i):=\sigma\bigl((x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\bigr),\qquad i=1,\ldots,n,

and suppose that the sequence of sigma-fields
\{\sigma_{x}(i)\}_{i=1}^{n}
is
\beta
-mixing.

Fix
s\in\{0,\ldots,S-1\}
. Then there exists a collection

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}:i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\Bigr\}

such that:

1.

for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
, the vector
(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
has the same distribution as
(x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
;

2.

the family

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}:i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\Bigr\}

is jointly independent;

3.

for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(x_{ij}^{*}\neq x_{ij}\text{ for some }j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

The same conclusion holds with
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
replaced by
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
.

Proof.

We prove the result for
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
; the proof for
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
is identical.

For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, define the past sigma-field

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{x}=\sigma\bigl((x_{tj})_{j=1}^{m_{t}}:t\leq i-S\bigr),

with the convention that
\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{x}
is trivial when
i-S\leq 0
.

For each
i
, let

\displaystyle E_{i}^{x}:=\prod_{t\leq i-S}\mathbb{R}^{m_{t}},\qquad F_{i}^{x}:=\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}},

and define the random elements

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\mathrm{past}}:=\Bigl((x_{tj})_{j=1}^{m_{t}}\Bigr)_{t\leq i-S}\in E_{i}^{x},\qquad\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{x}:=(x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\in F_{i}^{x}.

Since
E_{i}^{x}
is a countable product of Polish spaces and
F_{i}^{x}
is a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, both
E_{i}^{x}
and
F_{i}^{x}
are Polish spaces. Moreover,

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\mathrm{past}})=\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{x},\qquad\sigma(\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{x})=\sigma\bigl((x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\bigr).

Apply Theorem
15
to the
E_{i}^{x}\times F_{i}^{x}
-valued random element

\displaystyle(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\mathrm{past}},\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{x}).

Since the sequence
\{\sigma_{x}(i)\}
is
\beta
-mixing, we have

\displaystyle\beta\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{x},\sigma((x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}})\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

Also, by construction of the enlarged probability space,
\Delta_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
and
\Delta_{i}
is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\mathrm{past}},\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{x}).

Therefore, Theorem
15
yields an
F_{i}^{x}
-valued random element

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{x,*}:=(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}

and a measurable map

\displaystyle f_{i}^{x}:E_{i}^{x}\times F_{i}^{x}\times[0,1]\to F_{i}^{x}

such that

\displaystyle(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}=f_{i}^{x}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\mathrm{past}},(x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}},\Delta_{i}\bigr),

and:

1.

(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
has the same distribution as
(x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
;

2.

(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
is independent of
\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\mathrm{past}}
, hence independent of
\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{x}
;

3.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl((x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\neq(x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\bigr)=\beta\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{x},\sigma((x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}})\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(x_{ij}^{*}\neq x_{ij}\text{ for some }j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

(34)

Because the auxiliary variables
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
are i.i.d. and independent of the original process, each coupled block is generated using fresh external randomness.

We now prove joint independence on
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
.

Write the elements of
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
in increasing order as

\displaystyle i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{M}.

By construction of
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
,

\displaystyle i_{r+1}-i_{r}\geq 2S,\qquad r=1,\ldots,M-1.

We prove by induction on
m\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
that the family

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(x_{i_{r}j}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{r}}}:r=1,\ldots,m\Bigr\}

is jointly independent.

The case
m=1
is immediate.

Assume the claim holds for some
m<M
. We prove it for
m+1
.
Let

\displaystyle A_{r}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{m_{i_{r}}}),\qquad r=1,\ldots,m+1,

and write, for brevity,

\displaystyle x_{i_{r}}^{*}:=(x_{i_{r}j}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{r}}}.

Since

\displaystyle i_{m}\leq i_{m+1}-2S<i_{m+1}-S,

all original variables involved in the construction of

\displaystyle x_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,x_{i_{m}}^{*}

belong to the past up to time
i_{m+1}-S
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma(x_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,x_{i_{m}}^{*})\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{m}^{x},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{m}^{x}:=\sigma\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{x},\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}\bigr).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle x_{i_{m+1}}^{*}=f_{i_{m+1}}^{x}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\mathrm{past}},(x_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

By the coupling theorem,
x_{i_{m+1}}^{*}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{x}
.

Moreover, since the sequence
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
is i.i.d. and independent of the original process, the sigma-field

\displaystyle\sigma(\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}})

is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\mathrm{past}},(x_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

Since

\displaystyle x_{i_{m+1}}^{*}=f_{i_{m+1}}^{x}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\mathrm{past}},(x_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr),

it is measurable with respect to

\displaystyle\sigma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\mathrm{past}},(x_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

By construction, this sigma-field is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma(\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}),

and Theorem
15
ensures independence from

\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{x}
. Therefore,

x_{i_{m+1}}^{*}
is independent of

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{m}^{x}=\sigma\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{x},\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}\bigr),

and hence independent of
\sigma(x_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,x_{i_{m}}^{*})
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(x_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,x_{i_{m}}^{*}\in A_{m},x_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in A_{m+1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigl(x_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,x_{i_{m}}^{*}\in A_{m}\Bigr)\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(x_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in A_{m+1}\bigr).

By the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(x_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,x_{i_{m}}^{*}\in A_{m}\Bigr)=\prod_{r=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}\bigl(x_{i_{r}}^{*}\in A_{r}\bigr),

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(x_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,x_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in A_{m+1}\Bigr)=\prod_{r=1}^{m+1}\mathbb{P}\bigl(x_{i_{r}}^{*}\in A_{r}\bigr).

This proves that

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(x_{i_{r}j}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{r}}}:r=1,\ldots,m+1\Bigr\}

is jointly independent, completing the induction.

Thus the family

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}:i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\Bigr\}

is jointly independent, and for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
, the block
(x_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
has the same distribution as
(x_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(x_{ij}^{*}\neq x_{ij}\text{ for some }j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

This proves the lemma for
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
. The proof for
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
is identical.
∎

Lemma 17
.

Let

\displaystyle\sigma_{\epsilon}(i):=\sigma\bigl((\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\bigr),\qquad i=1,\ldots,n,

and suppose that the sequence of sigma-fields
\{\sigma_{\epsilon}(i)\}_{i=1}^{n}
is
\beta
-mixing.

Fix
s\in\{0,\ldots,S-1\}
. Then there exists a collection

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}:i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\Bigr\}

such that:

1.

for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
, the vector
(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
has the same distribution as
(\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
;

2.

the family

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}:i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\Bigr\}

is jointly independent;

3.

for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\neq\epsilon_{ij}\text{ for some }j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

The same conclusion holds with
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
replaced by
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
.

Proof.

We prove the result for
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
; the proof for
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
is identical.

For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, define the past sigma-field

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\epsilon}=\sigma\bigl((\epsilon_{tj})_{j=1}^{m_{t}}:t\leq i-S\bigr),

with the convention that
\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\epsilon}
is trivial when
i-S\leq 0
.

For each
i
, let

\displaystyle E_{i}^{\epsilon}:=\prod_{t\leq i-S}\mathbb{R}^{m_{t}},\qquad F_{i}^{\epsilon}:=\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}},

and define the random elements

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}}:=\Bigl((\epsilon_{tj})_{j=1}^{m_{t}}\Bigr)_{t\leq i-S}\in E_{i}^{\epsilon},\qquad\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\epsilon}:=(\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\in F_{i}^{\epsilon}.

Since
E_{i}^{\epsilon}
is a countable product of Polish spaces and
F_{i}^{\epsilon}
is a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, both
E_{i}^{\epsilon}
and
F_{i}^{\epsilon}
are Polish spaces.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}})=\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\epsilon},\qquad\sigma(\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\epsilon})=\sigma\bigl((\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\bigr).

Apply Theorem
15
to the
E_{i}^{\epsilon}\times F_{i}^{\epsilon}
-valued random element

\displaystyle(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}},\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\epsilon}).

Since the sequence of sigma-fields
\{\sigma_{\epsilon}(i)\}_{i=1}^{n}
is
\beta
-mixing, we have

\displaystyle\beta\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\epsilon},\sigma((\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}})\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

Also, by construction of the enlarged probability space,
\Delta_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
and
\Delta_{i}
is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}},\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\epsilon}).

Therefore, Theorem
15
yields an
F_{i}^{\epsilon}
-valued random element

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\epsilon,*}:=(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}

and a measurable map

\displaystyle f_{i}^{\epsilon}:E_{i}^{\epsilon}\times F_{i}^{\epsilon}\times[0,1]\to F_{i}^{\epsilon}

such that

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}=f_{i}^{\epsilon}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}},(\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}},\Delta_{i}\bigr),

and:

1.

(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
has the same distribution as
(\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
;

2.

(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
is independent of
\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}}
, hence independent of
\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\epsilon}
;

3.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl((\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\neq(\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\bigr)=\beta\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\epsilon},\sigma((\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}})\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\neq\epsilon_{ij}\text{ for some }j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

Because the auxiliary variables
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
are i.i.d. and independent of the original process, each coupled block is generated using fresh external randomness.

We now prove joint independence on
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
.

Write the elements of
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
in increasing order as

\displaystyle i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{M}.

By construction of
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
,

\displaystyle i_{r+1}-i_{r}\geq 2S,\qquad r=1,\ldots,M-1.

We prove by induction on
m\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
that the family

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(\epsilon_{i_{r}j}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{r}}}:r=1,\ldots,m\Bigr\}

is jointly independent.

The case
m=1
is immediate.

Assume the claim holds for some
m<M
. We prove it for
m+1
.
Let

\displaystyle A_{r}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{m_{i_{r}}}),\qquad r=1,\ldots,m+1,

and write, for brevity,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i_{r}}^{*}:=(\epsilon_{i_{r}j}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{r}}}.

Since

\displaystyle i_{m}\leq i_{m+1}-2S<i_{m+1}-S,

all original variables involved in the construction of

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{*}

belong to the past up to time
i_{m+1}-S
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma(\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{*})\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\epsilon},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\epsilon}:=\sigma\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\epsilon},\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}\bigr).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i_{m+1}}^{*}=f_{i_{m+1}}^{\epsilon}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}},(\epsilon_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

By the coupling theorem,
\epsilon_{i_{m+1}}^{*}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\epsilon}
.

Moreover, since the sequence
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
is i.i.d. and independent of the original process, the sigma-field

\displaystyle\sigma(\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}})

is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}},(\epsilon_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

Since

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i_{m+1}}^{*}=f_{i_{m+1}}^{\epsilon}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}},(\epsilon_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr),

it is measurable with respect to

\displaystyle\sigma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\epsilon,\mathrm{past}},(\epsilon_{i_{m+1}j})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{m+1}}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

By construction, this sigma-field is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma(\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}),

and Theorem
15
ensures independence from

\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\epsilon}
. Therefore,

\epsilon_{i_{m+1}}^{*}
is independent of

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\epsilon}=\sigma\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\epsilon},\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}\bigr),

and hence independent of
\sigma(\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{*})
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{*}\in A_{m},\epsilon_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in A_{m+1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{*}\in A_{m}\Bigr)\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\epsilon_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in A_{m+1}\bigr).

By the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{*}\in A_{m}\Bigr)=\prod_{r=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\epsilon_{i_{r}}^{*}\in A_{r}\bigr),

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{*}\in A_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in A_{m+1}\Bigr)=\prod_{r=1}^{m+1}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\epsilon_{i_{r}}^{*}\in A_{r}\bigr).

This proves that

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(\epsilon_{i_{r}j}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i_{r}}}:r=1,\ldots,m+1\Bigr\}

is jointly independent, completing the induction.

Thus the family

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}:i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\Bigr\}

is jointly independent, and for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
, the block
(\epsilon_{ij}^{*})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
has the same distribution as
(\epsilon_{ij})_{j=1}^{m_{i}}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\neq\epsilon_{ij}\text{ for some }j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

∎

Before stating the corresponding lemma for the spatial noise, we introduce the following definition.

Definition 1

(Independent and identically distributed random functions)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a Polish space of real-valued functions on
[0,1]^{d}
, equipped with its Borel sigma-field
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
. A collection of
\mathcal{H}
-valued random elements

\displaystyle\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{n}

is said to be independent and identically distributed if:

1.

the sigma-fields
\sigma(\delta_{1}),\ldots,\sigma(\delta_{n})
are independent;

2.

for every
A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
and every
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\delta_{i}\in A)=\mathbb{P}(\delta_{j}\in A).

Lemma 18
.

Let
\{\delta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be identically distributed random functions taking values in a Polish function space
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}))
, and suppose that the sequence of sigma-fields

\displaystyle\sigma_{\delta}(i):=\sigma(\delta_{i}),\qquad i=1,\ldots,n,

is
\beta
-mixing.

Fix
s\in\{0,\ldots,S-1\}
. Then there exists a collection

\displaystyle\{\delta_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}

such that:

1.

for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
, the random function
\delta_{i}^{*}
has the same distribution as
\delta_{i}
;

2.

the family

\displaystyle\{\delta_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}

is jointly independent;

3.

for each
i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\delta_{i}^{*}\neq\delta_{i})\leq\beta(S).

The same conclusion holds with
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
replaced by
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
.

Proof.

We prove the result for
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
; the proof for
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
is identical.

For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, define the past sigma-field

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\delta}=\sigma(\delta_{t}:t\leq i-S),

with the convention that
\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\delta}
is trivial when
i-S\leq 0
.

For each
i
, let

\displaystyle E_{i}^{\delta}:=\prod_{t\leq i-S}\mathcal{H},\qquad F_{i}^{\delta}:=\mathcal{H},

and define the random elements

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}}:=(\delta_{t})_{t\leq i-S}\in E_{i}^{\delta},\qquad\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\delta}:=\delta_{i}\in F_{i}^{\delta}.

Since
E_{i}^{\delta}
is a countable product of Polish spaces and
F_{i}^{\delta}=\mathcal{H}
is Polish, both
E_{i}^{\delta}
and
F_{i}^{\delta}
are Polish spaces. Here the index
t\leq i-S
indexes the coordinates of the product, so that

\displaystyle E_{i}^{\delta}=\left\{(h_{t})_{t\leq i-S}:h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}\text{ for all }t\leq i-S\right\}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}})=\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\delta},\qquad\sigma(\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\delta})=\sigma(\delta_{i}).

Apply Theorem
15
to the
E_{i}^{\delta}\times F_{i}^{\delta}
-valued random element

\displaystyle(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}},\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\delta}).

Since the sequence
\{\sigma_{\delta}(i)\}
is
\beta
-mixing, we have

\displaystyle\beta\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\delta},\sigma(\delta_{i})\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

Also, by construction of the enlarged probability space,
\Delta_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
and
\Delta_{i}
is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}},\mathbf{Y}_{i}^{\delta}).

Therefore, Theorem
15
yields an
F_{i}^{\delta}
-valued random element
\delta_{i}^{*}
, and a measurable map

\displaystyle f_{i}^{\delta}:E_{i}^{\delta}\times F_{i}^{\delta}\times[0,1]\to F_{i}^{\delta}

such that

\displaystyle\delta_{i}^{*}=f_{i}^{\delta}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}},\delta_{i},\Delta_{i}\bigr),

and:

1.

\delta_{i}^{*}
has the same distribution as
\delta_{i}
;

2.

\delta_{i}^{*}
is independent of
\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}}
, hence independent of
\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\delta}
;

3.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\delta_{i}^{*}\neq\delta_{i})=\beta\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i-S}^{\delta},\sigma(\delta_{i})\bigr)\leq\beta(S).

Because the auxiliary variables
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
are i.i.d. and independent of the original process, each coupled random function is generated using fresh external randomness.

We now prove joint independence on
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
.

Write the elements of
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
in increasing order as

\displaystyle i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{M}.

By construction of
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
,

\displaystyle i_{r+1}-i_{r}\geq 2S,\qquad r=1,\ldots,M-1.

We prove by induction on
m\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
that the family

\displaystyle\{\delta_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m}}^{*}\}

is jointly independent.

The case
m=1
is immediate.

Assume the claim holds for some
m<M
. We prove it for
m+1
.
Let

\displaystyle B_{r}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}),\qquad r=1,\ldots,m+1.

Since

\displaystyle i_{m}\leq i_{m+1}-2S<i_{m+1}-S,

all original random functions involved in the construction of

\displaystyle\delta_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m}}^{*}

belong to the past up to time
i_{m+1}-S
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma(\delta_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m}}^{*})\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\delta},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\delta}:=\sigma\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\delta},\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}\bigr).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}=f_{i_{m+1}}^{\delta}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}},\delta_{i_{m+1}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

By the coupling theorem,
\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\delta}
.

Moreover, since the sequence
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
is i.i.d. and independent of the original process, the sigma-field

\displaystyle\sigma(\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}})

is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}},\delta_{i_{m+1}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

Since

\displaystyle\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}=f_{i_{m+1}}^{\delta}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}},\delta_{i_{m+1}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr),

it is measurable with respect to

\displaystyle\sigma\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{i_{m+1}}^{\delta,\mathrm{past}},\delta_{i_{m+1}},\Delta_{i_{m+1}}\bigr).

By construction, this sigma-field is independent of

\displaystyle\sigma(\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}),

and Theorem
15
ensures independence from

\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\delta}
. Therefore,

\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}
is independent of

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\delta}=\sigma\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{i_{m+1}-S}^{\delta},\Delta_{i_{1}},\ldots,\Delta_{i_{m}}\bigr),

and hence independent of
\sigma(\delta_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m}}^{*})
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\delta_{i_{1}}^{*}\in B_{1},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m}}^{*}\in B_{m},\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in B_{m+1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl(\delta_{i_{1}}^{*}\in B_{1},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m}}^{*}\in B_{m}\bigr)\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in B_{m+1}\bigr).

By the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\delta_{i_{1}}^{*}\in B_{1},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m}}^{*}\in B_{m}\bigr)=\prod_{r=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\delta_{i_{r}}^{*}\in B_{r}\bigr),

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\delta_{i_{1}}^{*}\in B_{1},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\in B_{m+1}\bigr)=\prod_{r=1}^{m+1}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\delta_{i_{r}}^{*}\in B_{r}\bigr).

This proves that

\displaystyle\{\delta_{i_{1}}^{*},\ldots,\delta_{i_{m+1}}^{*}\}

is jointly independent, completing the induction.

Thus the family

\displaystyle\{\delta_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}

is jointly independent, each
\delta_{i}^{*}
has the same distribution as the corresponding
\delta_{i}
, and the coupling error satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\delta_{i}^{*}\neq\delta_{i})\leq\beta(S).

This proves the lemma for
