\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
. The proof for
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
is identical.

∎

S3.2.2
Deviation bounds for
\epsilon

Now we handle the general case, where
\epsilon^{*}
is the ghost version of
\epsilon
.

Let

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{mn}(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}.

To formalize the effective sample size arising from the blocking argument, for any subset

I\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}
, define the block empirical norm

\displaystyle\|g\|_{I,m}^{2}:=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\frac{1}{m_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij}).

For each block
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
and
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
, we use the shorthand

\displaystyle\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}^{2}:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}\frac{1}{m_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij}),\qquad\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}^{2}:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}\frac{1}{m_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij}).

Here
0\leq s\leq S-1
,
Since the families
\{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\}_{s=0}^{S-1}
and
\{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}\}_{s=0}^{S-1}
form a partition of
\{1,\dots,n\}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|g\|_{nm}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\Bigl(|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|\,\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}^{2}+|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|\,\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}^{2}\Bigr).

Corollary 19
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a function class such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{nm})\leq nm\,\phi_{nm}\Bigl(\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1})\Bigr),

(35)

and suppose that
\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\infty}(\tau)
, where
\tau\geq 1
. Let

\displaystyle S\geq\frac{2}{C_{\beta}}\log n.

Define

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n,S}(g):={}\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}{n}\,\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\sqrt{\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)\,\log\!\bigl(\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}^{-1}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|}{n}\,\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}\sqrt{\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)\,\log\!\bigl(\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}^{-1}\bigr)}.

Then for any
t\geq 1
, there exist positive constants
C_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
C_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{D^{\prime}_{mn}(g)}{C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\,t\,\Lambda_{n,S}(g)}\geq 1\right)\leq 2S\,C_{2}\exp\!\left(-ct^{2}\frac{nm}{S}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{4.3}\!\left(\frac{nm}{S}\right)\right)+n\,\beta(S).

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{mn}(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\left\{g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}-g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}.

(36)

Since

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\infty}(\tau)\subset B_{nm}(\tau),

it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\cap B_{nm}(\tau)=\mathcal{F}.

Step 1.

By Lemma
17
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigg(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\not=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\;\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigg(\epsilon_{ij}\not=\epsilon^{*}_{ij}\text{ for some }i\in\{1,\ldots,n\},\,j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\bigg(\epsilon_{ij}\not=\epsilon^{*}_{ij}\text{ for some }j\in\{1,\ldots,m_{i}\}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\;n\,\beta(S).

Step 2.

We rewrite the even and odd block terms as

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}=\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}}{n\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m_{i}}}g(x_{ij})\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}\epsilon_{ij}^{*},

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}=\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|}}{n\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m_{i}}}g(x_{ij})\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}\epsilon_{ij}^{*}.

Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\epsilon}_{ij}:=\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}\epsilon_{ij}^{*}.

(37)

This rescaling is introduced only to rewrite each block sum in the normalized form required by Corollary
6
. By Assumption 1
d
and
f
, the variables
\widetilde{\epsilon}_{ij}
are sub-Gaussian with parameter
\frac{1}{\sqrt{c}}\sigma_{\epsilon}
.

For each
s
, we apply Corollary
6
to the even block and to the odd block. Thus, for all
g\in\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\infty}(\tau)
, any
t>1
, and all
s
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m_{i}}}g(x_{ij})\widetilde{\epsilon}_{ij}

\displaystyle\leq\;\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m\,\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\Bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}^{-1})\Bigr\}}\frac{C_{1}}{c}\sigma_{\epsilon}\,t,

with probability at least

\displaystyle 1-C_{2}\exp\!\left(-ct^{2}|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m\,\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m)\right).

A completely analogous bound holds with
{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}
replaced by
{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}
.

Using a union bound over the
2S
block events, with probability at least

\displaystyle 1-2S\,C_{2}\exp\!\left(-ct^{2}\frac{nm}{S}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{4.3}\!\left(\frac{nm}{S}\right)\right),

we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}}{n\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m_{i}}}g(x_{ij})\widetilde{\epsilon}_{ij}+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|}}{n\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m_{i}}}g(x_{ij})\widetilde{\epsilon}_{ij}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{c}\sigma_{\epsilon}t\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}{n}\Biggl[\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\sqrt{\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\Bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}^{-1})\Bigr\}}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C_{1}}{c}\sigma_{\epsilon}t\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|}{n}\Biggl[\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}\sqrt{\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}\Bigl\{\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}^{-1})\Bigr\}}\Biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}}{c}\sigma_{\epsilon}t\,\Lambda_{n,S}(g).

Hence, for a suitable positive constant
C_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\,t\,\Lambda_{n,S}(g),

with probability at least

\displaystyle 1-2S\,C_{2}\exp\!\left(-ct^{2}\frac{nm}{S}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{4.3}\!\left(\frac{nm}{S}\right)\right).

Combining this with Step 1 yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\cdot\mid x}\left(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{D^{\prime}_{mn}(g)}{C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\sigma_{\epsilon}\,t\,\Lambda_{n,S}(g)}\geq 1\right)\leq 2S\,C_{2}\exp\!\left(-ct^{2}\frac{nm}{S}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{4.3}\!\left(\frac{nm}{S}\right)\right)+n\,\beta(S).

This completes the proof.
∎

S3.2.3
Coupling Error in Temporal-spatial Model

Now we handle the general case, where
x^{*}
is the ghost version of
x
.
Let

\displaystyle\left\|g\right\|_{nm}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij}).

Lemma 20
.

Suppose
g\in\mathcal{F}
implies
\left\|g\right\|_{\infty}\leq\tau
.
For any
\eta\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
, there exists a constant
C_{\eta}
only depending on
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}-\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\bigr|\bigr)\leq 12.

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}-\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{{\mathcal{L}_{2}}}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})\bigr\}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})-\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}

(38)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})\bigr\}+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\left\{g^{2}\left(x_{ij}^{*}\right)-\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\right\}.

Step 1.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})\bigr\}}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\big|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\big|g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})\big|}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\mathbb{I}_{\bigl\{x_{ij}=x_{ij}^{*}\bigr\}}\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\bigl|g^{2}\bigl(x_{ij}\bigr)-g^{2}\bigl(x_{ij}^{*}\bigr)\bigr|}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\mathbb{I}_{\{x_{ij}\not=x_{ij}^{*}\}}\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{\bigl|g^{2}(x_{ij})-g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})\bigr|}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 0+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{I}_{\{x_{ij}\not=x_{ij}^{*}\}}\frac{2\tau^{2}}{\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\right)\leq 2,

where the last inequality follows from (
34
).

Step 2.
Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})-\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)}\bigr|\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}\log^{5.3}(|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m)}\bigr|\bigr)\leq\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|12}{n},

where the second last inequality follows from (
33
) which implies
|{\mathcal{J}}_{e,s}|\asymp n/S
as well as
|{\mathcal{J}}_{o,s}|\asymp n/S
, indeed, we have
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|\leq\frac{K+2}{2}\leq\frac{n}{2S}+1\leq\frac{n}{S}
, and the last inequality follows from applying

Corollary

10
in
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
block.
So

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\big|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\bigl\{g^{2}(x_{ij}^{*})-\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}}{\eta\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{5.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\big|\bigr)\leq\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|12}{n}+\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|12}{n}

\displaystyle\leq 12.

Step 3.
Therefore
(
38
) and Step 1 gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}}\bigl|\frac{\|g\|_{nm}^{2}-\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}}{\eta\bigl\|g\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\tau C_{\eta}\phi_{nm/S}\log^{4.3}(nm/S)+\tau^{2}\beta(S)}\bigr|\bigr)\leq 14.

∎

S4
Sketch of Proof Of Neural Network Approximation with Independent Observations (Corollary 3 in the Main Manuscript)

The proof is similar to Step 1 to Step 4 of proof of Theorem 1 in Section
S2
, except no terms for
\beta
-mixing needed.
By the same argument in Section
S2

Lemma

2
,
we can choose
\mathcal{A}_{nm}=\max\left\{\sigma_{\epsilon},\sigma_{\gamma}\right\}o\left(\log(nm)\right)
.
Thus, it is true that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\phi_{nm}+2\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}+Ca_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm),

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{nm}^{2}\leq\bigl(2C_{3}+1\bigr)\bigl\{a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}\bigl(nm\bigr)+\frac{1}{n}\bigr\},

hold with high probability with respect to the distribution of
\{x_{ij}\}^{n,m_{i}}_{i=1,j=1}
, which gives

\displaystyle a_{n}\left(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}+1\right)\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}\left(\frac{1}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\log^{6.3}(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}

holds with probability approaches to one.

S5
Sketch of Proof of Neural Network Approximation with Temporal Dependence (Corollary 4 in the Main Manuscript)

The proof of Corollary 4 in the Main Manuscript adheres to a similar trajectory, with the incorporation of simpler step into the analysis of Theorem 1.
The
m_{i}=1
, in which the set of designs
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
is replaced by the set of designs
\left\{x_{ij}\right\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}

For
1\leq i\leq n
, let

\displaystyle y_{i}=f^{*}(x_{i})+\epsilon_{i}.

We utilize the following block scheme for
\beta
-mixing conditions.
Let
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a collection of stationary
\beta
-mixing time series with mixing coefficient
\beta(s)
.
For any
S\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and

K=\lfloor n/S\rfloor
, denote

{\mathcal{I}}_{k}=\begin{cases}(0+(k-1)S,\ kS]&\text{when }1\leq k\leq K,\\
(KS,n]&\text{when }k=K+1.\end{cases}

By
Theorem

15
, for any
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}
, there exists
\{x_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(x_{i}\not=x^{*}_{i})=\beta(S)

(39)

and that
x_{i}^{*}
is independent of
\sigma(\{x_{j}\}_{j\leq i-1}).

Denote for
0\leq s\leq S-1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{e,s}=\{\,i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}\text{ for }k\equiv 0\pmod{2}\text{ and }i\equiv s\pmod{S}\,\}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{o,s}=\{\,i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}\text{ for }k\equiv 1\pmod{2}\text{ and }i\equiv s\pmod{S}\,\}.

Then

\displaystyle\frac{k-1}{2}\leq|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|\leq\frac{k+2}{2}\text{ and }\frac{k-1}{2}\leq|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|\leq\frac{k+2}{2}

(40)

and that

\displaystyle\bigcup_{s=0}^{S-1}\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\cup\bigcup_{s=0}^{S-1}\mathcal{J}_{o,s}=[1,\ldots,n].

Therefore,

\{x_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in{\mathcal{J}}_{e,l}}
are i.i.d. random variables.

Remark 2
.

The same analysis in Supplementary Material
S2

Lemma

2
shows that
by choosing
\mathcal{A}_{n}=o(\log(n))
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\epsilon_{i}\right|\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{n}}{4}\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq n\right)=1-o(1).

Then, with the deviation result as a special case of Section
S3.1
and
S3.2
by letting
m_{i}=1
. We can show that

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{f}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|f^{*}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{n}}{4}+\sqrt{2\phi_{n}}\leq\mathcal{A}_{n},

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\|_{n}^{2}+Ca_{n}{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\phi_{\frac{n}{S}}\log^{5.3}(\frac{n}{S})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{2}\beta(S),

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\|_{n}^{2}\leq(C_{3}+1)a_{n}{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\phi_{\frac{n}{S}}\log^{5.3}(n),

by combining the above steps.

Note that

\phi_{n}=\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}\left(n^{-\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\right)\leq\phi_{\frac{n}{S}}=\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}(\frac{n}{S})^{-\frac{2p}{2p+K}}
,
and by by Assumption 1
a
for
\beta(S)
and replacing
S=O(\log(n))
,
with high probability with respect to the probability measure on
\{x_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
, gives error rate

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}-f^{*}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{n}\phi_{\frac{n}{S}}\log^{5.3}(n)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{2}\beta(S)\lesssim a_{n}(\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}+1)\bigl(\frac{1}{n}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\log^{7.3}(n).

S6
Proof of Theorem 5

S6.1
Preliminary Results for Proof of Theorem 5

Lemma 21

(Neural Network Approximation Result on Manifold, Theorem 2 of
Kohler et al. (2023)
)
.

Let
f^{*}
be
(p,C)
-smooth
function on a
d^{*}
-dimensional Lipschitz-manifold.
There
exists a neural network
f
with the the choice of
L=L_{nm}
and
r=r_{nm}
, where

\displaystyle L=L_{nm}\asymp\log(nm)\quad\text{and}\quad r=r_{nm}\asymp\left(nm\right)^{\frac{d^{*}}{2(2p+d^{*})}}

\displaystyle\text{ or},L=L_{nm}\asymp\log(nm)\cdot\bigl(nm\bigr)^{\frac{d^{*}}{2(2p+d^{*})}}\quad\text{and}\quad r=r_{nm}\asymp 1

(41)

satisfying that

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)}\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}\leq\sqrt{\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}},

(42)

where

\displaystyle\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}=(nm)^{\frac{-2p}{(2p+d^{*})}},

when
n
is sufficiently large.

Thus, by the choice of
L
and
r
in (
41
), we have

\displaystyle Lr\asymp\log(nm)(nm)^{\frac{d^{*}}{2(2p+d^{*})}},

\displaystyle Lr^{2}\lesssim\log(nm)\left[\left(nm\right)^{\frac{d^{*}}{2(2p+d^{*})}}\right]^{2}\lesssim nm.

(43)

Lemma 22
.

For any fixed
\mathcal{A}_{nm}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}})=\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}).

Furthermore, with
L,r
satisfying (
41
), then

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}})\lesssim nm\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}\log^{3.3}(nm)\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}).

Proof.

Recall the probability distribution is supported on a manifold
\mathcal{M}
, so we write
\|f\|_{nm}
as
\|f\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}}
.
Then by the same argument as in the Euclidean space from
Lemma

24
, it follows that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}})=\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}).

Then, with the choice of
L
and
r
in (
43
) leads to

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}})\lesssim nm\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}\log^{3.3}(nm)\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}).

∎

S6.2
Proof of Theorem 5

Proof.

Let

\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
be such that

\displaystyle\left\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\right\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}^{2}\leq 2\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}},

(44)

where the existence of
\overline{f}
is guaranteed by
Lemma

21
.

The same analysis in Supplementary Material
S2

Lemma

2
shows that
by choosing

\mathcal{A}_{nm}\asymp\max\left\{\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{nm},\sigma_{\gamma}\cdot\mathcal{C}_{n}\right\}\asymp\max\left\{\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot\sqrt{\log(nm)},\sigma_{\gamma}\cdot\sqrt{\log(n)}\right\}
, or
\max\{\sigma_{\epsilon},\sigma_{\gamma}\}\cdot o(\log(nm))
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\bigl|\epsilon_{ij}\bigr|\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq n,\,1\leq j\leq m_{i}\,\text{and}\,\bigl\|\gamma_{i}\bigr\|_{\infty,nm}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq n\bigr)

\displaystyle=\ 1-o(1).

Since
\mathcal{A}_{nm}
can be chosen appropriately large with respect to
n
while
\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}
decays with respect to
n
and
m
, assume that
\sqrt{\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}}\leq\frac{3}{4}\mathcal{A}_{nm}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\overline{f}\bigr\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}\leq\bigl\|f^{*}\bigr\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}+\bigl\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\bigr\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}\leq\frac{{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}}{4}+\sqrt{2\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}}\leq{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}.

It follows that
\overline{f}=\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
.
Since

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\bigl\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\bigl\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\phi_{nm,\mathcal{M}}+2\left\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},

(45)

it suffices to show

\displaystyle\bigl\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim a_{n}{\mathcal{A}^{2}_{nm}}\phi_{\frac{nm}{S},\mathcal{M}}\log^{5.3}(nm)+a_{n}{\mathcal{A}^{2}_{nm}}\beta(S)+\frac{1}{n},

where
S\asymp\log(n)
.

With
Lemma

22
established, we follow a similar approach as in Steps 1 to 4 from Supplementary Material Section
S2
, adapting the deviation bounds to the manifold setting. For positive constants
C
and
C_{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+Ca_{n}\bigl\{\phi_{\frac{nm}{S},\mathcal{M}}\log^{5.3}(nm)+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{nm}\beta(S)\bigr\},

(46)

and

\displaystyle\bigl\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\bigr\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}}^{2}\leq(2C_{3}+1)\bigl\{a_{n}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{nm}\phi_{\frac{nm}{S},\mathcal{M}}\log^{5.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{nm}\beta(S)+\frac{1}{n}\bigr\}.

(47)

Combining (
45
), (
46
) and (
47
), which hold with high probability,
we establish Equation (12) in the Main Manuscript.

∎

S7
Proof of Lemma 4 in the Main Manuscript

Proof.

First, we consider the simpler model where
\gamma_{i}(x)=0
for all
i\in[n],
and
x\in[0,1]
, i.e., there is no spatial noise. This is,

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij})+\epsilon_{ij}.

Recall that
\mathcal{H}(l,\mathcal{P})
denotes the space of hierarchical composition
model in Definition 3 in the Main Manuscript. Consider the case

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}=\left\{\left(p_{1},K_{1}\right),\left(p_{2},K_{2}\right),\ldots,\left(p_{l},K_{l}\right)\right\},

where

p_{i}\in[1,\infty)
and
K_{i}\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}
for every

i\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
, with

K_{l}\leq K_{l-1}\leq\cdots\leq K_{1}\leq d
.
By
Lemma

23
, there exists
C_{\text{opt,1}}
, a positive constant, such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\inf_{\widetilde{f}}\sup_{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\bigl\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}>C_{\text{opt,1}}\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}(nm)^{-2p/(2p+K)}\bigr)>0.

It now suffices to show that there exist a constant
C_{\text{opt,2}}>0,
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\inf_{\widetilde{f}}\sup_{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}>C_{\text{opt,2}}n^{-1}\right)>0.

To this end, we assume that
\epsilon_{ij}=0,\ \forall i=1,...,n;j=1,...,m_{i}.
Therefore, we obtain a simple functional mean model given by,

\displaystyle y_{ij}(x_{ij})=X(x_{ij})=f^{*}(x_{ij})+\gamma_{i}(x_{ij}),i\in[n],\,j\in[m_{i}].

(48)

Here
\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
denotes the spatial noise and follow Assumption 1 in the Main Manuscript.

Consider
S=\frac{1}{C_{\beta}}\log^{(2p+K)/2p}(n)
with
C_{\beta}>0
a constant stated in Assumption 1 in the Main Manuscript. Take into account the copies of the design points and spatial noises, denoted as
\{x_{ij}^{*}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
and
\{\gamma_{i}^{*}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, which were generated in Supplementary Material
S3.2
. During the formation of these copies in Supplementary Material
S3.2
, the quantities
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|
and
|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|
, which represent the count of even and odd blocks respectively, satisfy
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|,|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|\asymp n/S
. Thus, there exist positive constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
such that

\displaystyle c_{1}n/S\leq|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|,|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|\leq c_{2}n/S.

(49)

By Lemma
16
and Lemma
17
, we have that
\{x_{ij}^{*}\}_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s},j\in[m_{i}]}
and
\{\gamma_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}
are independent. Further, for any
i\in[n]
it follows that
\mathbb{P}(x_{ij}\neq x_{ij}^{*}\ \text{for some}\ j\in[m_{i}])\leq\beta_{x}(S).
Moreover, by Assumption 1 in the Main Manuscript and that
S=\frac{1}{C_{\beta}}\log^{(2p+K)/2p}(n)\geq\frac{2}{C_{\beta}}\log(n)
, we note that
\mathbb{P}(x_{ij}\neq x_{ij}^{*}\ \text{for some}\ j\in[m_{i}])\leq\beta_{x}(S)\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}.
Therefore, the event
\Omega_{x}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\{x_{ij}=x_{ij}^{*}\ \forall\ j\in[m_{i}]\}
happens with probability at least
\frac{1}{n}
. The same is satisfied for
\{\gamma_{i}^{*}\}_{i=1}^{n}
with
\Omega_{\gamma}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\{\gamma_{i}=\gamma_{i}^{*}\}
. We denote by
\Omega
the event
\Omega_{x}\cap\Omega_{\gamma}.

Let
\widetilde{f}
be an estimator for the model described in Equation (
48
) based on the observations
\{(x_{ij},y_{ij})\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
.
Let
s\in[S]
. We notice that
\widetilde{f}
is an estimator for the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij})+\gamma_{i}(x_{ij}),\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}].

Denote by
\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}
, the estimator such that,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}=\mathop{\mathrm{arginf}}_{\widehat{f}}\|\widehat{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

which applies to all estimators of the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\gamma_{i}^{*}(x_{ij}^{*}),\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}].

(50)

Specifically,
\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}
is the estimator derived from the data
x_{ij}^{*}
and
\gamma_{i}^{*}
for
f^{*}
, with
(i,j)\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\times[m_{i}]
.
Under the event
\Omega
,
\widetilde{f}
is an estimator for the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\gamma_{i}^{*}(x_{ij}^{*}),\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}].

Now observe that when looking for the minimax lower bound for the model described in Equation (
50
), for each time
i
only one observation among the
m_{i}
available observations contributes to the determination of such minimax lower bound. To see this, suppose that conditioning in
x_{ij}^{*}
, we examine the distributions

P_{1}
and
P_{2}
originating from
Z_{i,1}=(z_{i,1},...,z_{i,1})^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
and
Z_{i,2}=(z_{i,1},...,z_{i,1})^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
, where
z_{i,l}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu_{l},1)
for
l=1
and
l=2.
Note that the Kullback-Leibler divergence
KL(P_{1},P_{2})
is solely dependent on
KL(\mathcal{N}(\mu_{1},1),\mathcal{N}(\mu_{2},1))
. By invoking Le Cam’s lemma, the problem reduces to the estimation of the mean based on
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|
observations. Given the independence of the copies of the design points and spatial noises, it is established that the minimax lower bound for this task is
\frac{1}{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|}\bigr)\,>0,\,

for a positive constant
C_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}.

Assume that
Cm\geq m_{i}\geq cm,
for a positive constant
c,C
, and
m=\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{m_{i}}\Big)^{-1}
. From this fact and Inequality (
49
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}S}{Cc_{2}n}\bigr)\,>C_{0}>0.\,

(51)

for a constant
C_{0}.

Similarly, let

\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}
be the estimator such that,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}=\mathop{\mathrm{arginf}}_{\widehat{f}}\|\widehat{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

which applies to all estimators of the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\gamma_{i}^{*}(x_{ij}^{*}),\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}].

Similarly, under the event
\Omega
,
\widetilde{f}
is an estimator for the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\gamma_{i}^{*}(x_{ij}^{*}),\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

with

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}S}{Cc_{2}n}\bigr)\,>C_{0}>0,\,

(52)

for a positive constant
C_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}.

We are now going to elaborate on the final observations of this discussion. Let
C_{\text{opt,2}}=\frac{1}{Cc_{2}}\min_{s\in[S]}\{{C_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},C_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}\}
. Then,

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,2}}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,2}}}{n}\Big\}\cap\Omega\bigr)>0.

The logic behind the second inequality is as follows. For any
f^{*}\in\mathcal{F},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,2}}}{n}\Big\}\cap\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2}}}{n}\Big\}\cap\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\bigcup_{s=1}^{S}\Big\{\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{Cc_{2}n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\bigcup\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}{Cc_{2}n}\Big\}\Big\}\cap\Omega\bigr)

\displaystyle>C_{0},

(53)

where the first inequality is followed by the fact that
S=\frac{1}{C_{\beta}}\log^{(2p+K)/2p}(n)\geq 1.
Moreover the third inequality is achieved from Inequality (
51
) and (
52
). The derivation of the second inequality is explained below.

Under the event
\Omega
the relation,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\geq\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\Big\},

holds for any
s\in[S].
These specific conditions allow us to conclude that the event
\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}<\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2}}}{n}\}
is contained in the event

\displaystyle\bigcap_{s=1}^{S}\Big\{\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{Cc_{2}n}\Big\}\bigcap\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}{Cc_{2}n}\Big\}\Big\},

and consequently the second inequality in Inequality (
53
) is satisfied. Specifically, suppose that
\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,<\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2}}}{n}
. For any
s\in[S]
by the definition of
C_{\text{opt,2}}
we have that
\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,<\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{Cc_{2}n}
. It follows that
\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{Cc_{2}n}
. Similarly,
\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,<\,\frac{SC_{\text{opt,2},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}{Cc_{2}n}
, concluding the contention of the aforementioned events.

The claim is then followed by taking
C_{\text{opt}}=\frac{1}{2}\min\{C_{\text{opt,1}},C_{\text{opt,2}}\}
.
To see this, we think two cases.
For case 1 where
\frac{1}{n}\geq(nm)^{-2p/(2p+K)}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\text{opt}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\leq\frac{C_{\text{opt}}}{n}\leq\frac{C_{\text{opt,2}}}{2n},\text{ so }C_{\text{opt}}\bigl(\frac{1}{n}\,+\,\bigl(\frac{1}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\bigr)\leq\frac{C_{\text{opt,2}}}{n},

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,C_{\text{opt}}\bigl(\frac{1}{n}\,+\,\bigl(\frac{1}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\bigr)\bigr)\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,2}}}{n}\bigr)>0.

For case 2 where
\frac{1}{n}<(nm)^{-2p/(2p+K)}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\text{opt}}}{n}\leq\frac{C_{\text{opt}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\leq\frac{C_{\text{opt,1}}}{2(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}},

so

\displaystyle C_{\text{opt}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\,+\,\left(\frac{1}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\right)\leq\frac{C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}},

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,C_{\text{opt}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\,+\,\left(\frac{1}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\right)>0.

Since the above condition holds for any
(p,k)\in\mathcal{P}
, it leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,C_{\text{opt}}\bigl(\frac{1}{n}\,+\,\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}\left(\frac{1}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+K}}\bigr)\bigr)>0.

∎

Lemma 23
.

Consider the simpler model where
\delta_{i}(x)=0
for all
i\in[n],
and
x\in[0,1]
, i.e., there is no spatial noise. This is,

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij})+\epsilon_{ij}.

Recall that
\mathcal{H}(l,\mathcal{P})
denotes the space of hierarchical composition
model in Definition 3 in the Main Manuscript.
Consider the case

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}=\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}:=\left\{\left(p_{1},K_{1}\right),\left(p_{2},K_{2}\right),\ldots,\left(p_{l},K_{l}\right)\right\},

where
p_{i}\in[1,\infty)
and
K_{i}\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}
for every

i\in\{1,\ldots,l\}
, with

K_{l}\leq K_{l-1}\leq\cdots\leq K_{1}\leq d
. There exists
C_{\text{opt,1}}
, a positive constant, such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\inf_{\widetilde{f}}\sup_{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\bigl\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}>C_{\text{opt,1}}\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}(nm)^{-2p/(2p+K)}\bigr)>0.

Proof.

A direct consequence of
Schmidt-Hieber (2020)
is the following. Let
\{x_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}\subset[0,1]^{d}
denoting the random locations from Assumption 1 in the Main Manuscript, where the temporal-spatial data
\{y_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}},
defined by

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij})+\epsilon_{ij},

(54)

is observed. Here
\{\epsilon_{ij}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
denotes the measurements error and follow Assumption 1 in the Main Manuscript. Consider
S=\frac{1}{C_{\beta}}\log^{(2p+K)/2p}(n)
with
C_{\beta}>0
a constant stated in Assumption 1 in the Main Manuscript. Take into account the copies of the design points and measurement errors, denoted as
\{x_{ij}^{*}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
and
\{\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
, which were generated in Supplementary Material
S3.2
. During the formation of these copies in Supplementary Material
S3.2
, the quantities
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|
and
|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|
, which represent the count of even and odd blocks respectively, satisfy
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|,|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|\asymp n/S
. Thus, there exist positive constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
such that

\displaystyle c_{1}n/S\leq|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|,|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|\leq c_{2}n/S.

(55)

By Lemma
16
and Lemma
17
, we have that
\{x_{ij}^{*}\}_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s},j\in[m_{i}]}
and
\{\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\}_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{o,s},j\in[m_{i}]}
are independent. Further, for any
i\in[n]
it follows that
\mathbb{P}(x_{ij}\neq x_{ij}^{*}\ \text{for some}\ j\in[m_{i}])\leq\beta_{x}(S).
Moreover, by Assumption 1 in the Main Manuscript and that
S=\frac{1}{C_{\beta}}\log^{(2p+K)/2p}(n)\geq\frac{2}{C_{\beta}}\log(n)
, we note that
\mathbb{P}(x_{ij}\neq x_{ij}^{*}\ \text{for some}\ j\in[m_{i}])\leq\beta_{x}(S)\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}.
Therefore, the event
\Omega_{x}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\{x_{ij}=x_{ij}^{*}\ \forall\ j\in[m_{i}]\}
happens with probability at least
\frac{1}{n}
. The same is satisfied for
\{\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
with
\Omega_{\epsilon}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\{\epsilon_{ij}=\epsilon_{ij}^{*}\ \forall\ j\in[m_{i}]\}
. We denote by
\Omega
the event
\Omega_{x}\cap\Omega_{\epsilon}.

Let
\widetilde{f}
be an estimator for the model described in Equation (
54
) based on the observations
\{(x_{ij},y_{ij})\}_{i=1,j=1}^{n,m_{i}}
.

Let
s\in[S]
. We notice that
\widetilde{f}
is an estimator for the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij})+\epsilon_{ij},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}].

Denote by
\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}
, the estimator such that,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}=\mathop{\mathrm{arginf}}_{\widehat{f}}\|\widehat{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

which applies to all estimators of the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\epsilon_{ij}^{*},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}].

Specifically,
\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}
is the estimator derived from the data
x_{ij}^{*}
and
\epsilon_{ij}^{*}
for
f^{*}
,
with
(i,j)\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}\times[m_{i}]
.
Under the event
\Omega
,
\widetilde{f}
is an estimator for the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\epsilon_{ij}^{*},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

Using the independence of the copies of the design points and measurement errors, a direct consequence of
Schmidt-Hieber (2020)
is that

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}m_{i}\Big)^{-2p/(2p+K)}\bigr)\,>0,\,

for a positive constant
C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}.
Thus, for any
(p,k)\in\mathcal{P}
,

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}m_{i}\Big)^{-2p/(2p+K)}\bigr)\,>0.

Assume that
Cm\geq m_{i}\geq cm,
for a positive constant
c,C
, and
m=\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{m_{i}}\Big)^{-1}
. From this fact and Inequality (
55
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}S^{2p/(2p+K)}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\bigr)\,>C_{0}>0.\,

(56)

for a constant
C_{0}.

Similarly, let

\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}
be the estimator such that,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}=\mathop{\mathrm{arginf}}_{\widehat{f}}\|\widehat{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

which applies to all estimators of the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\epsilon_{ij}^{*},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

with

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}S^{2p/(2p+K)}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\bigr)\,>C_{0}>0,\,

(57)

for a positive constant
C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}.

Under the event
\Omega
,

\widetilde{f}
is an estimator for the model

\displaystyle y_{ij}=f^{*}(x_{ij}^{*})+\epsilon_{ij}^{*},\ i\in{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}},\ j\in[m_{i}],

We are now going to elaborate on the final observations of this discussion. Let

\displaystyle C_{\text{opt,1}}=\frac{1}{(Cc_{2})^{2p/(2p+K)}}\min_{s\in[S]}\{{C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}},C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}\}.

Then,

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\right)

(58)

\displaystyle\geq\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\Big\}\cap\Omega\bigr)>0.

The logic behind the second inequality is as follows. For any
f^{*}\in\mathcal{F},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\Big\}\cap\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\Big\}\cap\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\bigcup_{l=1}^{S}\Big\{\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\Big\}\bigcup\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

(59)

\displaystyle\left.\,\geq\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\Big\}\Big\}\cap\Omega\right)

\displaystyle>C_{0},

(60)

where the first inequality is followed by the fact that
S=\frac{1}{C_{\beta}}\log^{(2p+K)/2p}(n)\geq 1.
Moreover the third inequality is achieved from Inequality (
56
) and (
57
). The derivation of the second inequality is explained below.

Under the event
\Omega
the relation,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\geq\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\Big\},

holds for any
s\in[S].
These specific conditions allow us to conclude that the event
\{\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}<\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\}
is contained in the event

\displaystyle\bigcap_{s=1}^{S}\Big\{\,

\displaystyle\Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\bigcap\ \Big\{\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}\Big\}\,\Big\}

and consequently the second inequality in Inequality (
59
) is satisfied. Specifically, suppose that
\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1}}}{(nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}
. For any
s\in[S]
by the definition of
C_{\text{opt,1}}
we have that
\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}
. It follows that
\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{e,s}}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}
. Similarly,
\|\widetilde{f}^{\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,<\,\frac{S^{2p/(2p+K)}C_{\text{opt,1},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}}}{(Cc_{2}nm)^{2p/(2p+K)}}
, concluding the contention of the aforementioned events.

Since (
58
) holds for any
(p,k)\in\mathcal{P}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim\sup}\,\underset{f^{*}\in\mathcal{H}(l,\widetilde{\mathcal{P}})}{\sup}\,\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|\widetilde{f}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\,\geq\,C_{\text{opt,1}}\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}(nm)^{-2p/(2p+K)}\bigr)>0.

∎

S8
Additional Technical Lemmas

Lemma 24
.

For any fixed
\mathcal{A}_{nm}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1})

Furthermore, with
L,r
satisfying (
6
)

Lr\asymp\log\left(nm\right)\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}\left(nm\right)^{\frac{K}{2(2p+K)}}
and

Lr^{2}\lesssim\log\left(nm\right)\left[\max_{(p,K)\in\mathcal{P}}\left(nm\right)^{\frac{K}{2(2p+K)}}\right]^{2}\lesssim nm
, we have

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})\lesssim nm\phi_{nm}\log^{3.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1}).

Proof.

It follows from Theorem 6 of

Bartlett et al. (2019)
(see also the proof of Lemma 19 on page 78 of
Kohler and Langer (2021)
) that
the VC-dimension of
\mathcal{F}(L,r)
satisfies

\displaystyle\text{VC}(\mathcal{F}(L,r))\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2}).

By a slight variant of Lemma 19 of
Kohler and Langer (2021)
, where
\mathcal{L}_{1}
norm is replaced by
\mathcal{L}_{2}
norm (which is implied by Lemma 9.2 (
Lemma

26
) and Theorem 9.4 (
Lemma

27
) of

Györfi et al. (2002)
), it follows that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\bigl\{\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\delta^{-2})+\log\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\delta^{-2})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}).

Furthermore, with the choice of
L
and
r
,

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log({\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\lesssim nm\phi_{nm}\log^{3}(nm)\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle=nm\phi_{nm}\log^{3}(nm)\left[\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm})+\log(\delta^{-1})\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim nm\phi_{nm}\log^{3}(nm)\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm})\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\lesssim nm\phi_{nm}\log^{3}(nm)\log(\log(nm))\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\lesssim nm\phi_{nm}\log^{3.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1}).

∎

Lemma 25
.

For non-random function
g^{*}
, denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}=\left\{f_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-g^{*}:f\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)\right\}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}},\|\cdot\|_{nm})\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}).

Furthermore, if the probability distribution
is supported on some manifold
\mathcal{M}
, then it also holds that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}},\|\cdot\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}})\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log(\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}).

Proof.

It suffices to observe that

(f_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-g^{*})-(\tilde{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-g^{*})=f_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\tilde{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
, for any
f
and
\tilde{f}
in
\mathcal{F}(L,r)
. Thus, the distance between any two functions in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
is the same as the distance between the corresponding functions in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r)
.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}},\|\cdot\|_{nm})=\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log({\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\delta^{-1}),

where the last inequality follows from the proof in
Lemma

24
.

Furthermore, recall the probability distribution is supported on a manifold
\mathcal{M}
, we write
\|f\|_{nm}
as
\|f\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}}
.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}},\|\cdot\|_{nm,\mathcal{M}})

\displaystyle=\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}},\|\cdot\|_{nm})

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{2}r^{2}\log(Lr^{2})\log({\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\delta^{-1}).

∎

Lemma 26

(Lemma 9.2,
Györfi et al. (2002)
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a class of functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
\nu
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d},p\geq 1
and
\epsilon>0
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\bigl(2\epsilon,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}(\nu)}\bigr)\leq\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}(\nu)}\bigr)\leq\mathcal{M}\bigl(\epsilon,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{L_{p}(\nu)}\bigr).

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{p}\left(2\epsilon,\mathcal{G},z_{1}^{n}\right)\leq\mathcal{N}_{p}\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{G},z_{1}^{n}\right)\leq\mathcal{M}_{p}\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{G},z_{1}^{n}\right)

for all
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Lemma 27

(Theorem 9.4,
Györfi et al. (2002)
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a class of functions
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,B]
with
V_{\mathcal{G}^{+}}\geq 2
, let
p\geq 1
, let
\nu
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
0<\epsilon<\frac{B}{4}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\nu)}\right)\leq 3\left(\frac{2eB^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\frac{3eB^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)^{V_{\mathcal{G}^{+}}}.

Lemma 28
.

Suppose Assumption 1 holds. Let
\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma_{i}
are identically distributed. Then

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}(\langle\gamma_{0},\gamma_{i}\rangle)\big|\leq 2\beta^{1/r}(i)\mathbb{E}(\|\gamma_{0}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q})^{1/q}

for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
q,r
such that
q^{-1}+r^{-1}=1
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

15
, there exists
\gamma_{i}^{*}
such that
\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma_{i}^{*}
are independent, that
\gamma_{i}^{*}
are identically distributed to
\gamma_{i}
, and that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\gamma_{i}^{*}\not=\gamma_{i})\leq\beta(i).

Observe that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}(\langle\gamma_{0},\gamma_{i}\rangle)\big|=\big|\mathbb{E}(\langle\gamma_{0},\gamma_{i}-\gamma_{i}^{*}\rangle)+\mathbb{E}(\langle\gamma_{0},\gamma_{i}^{*}\rangle)\big|.

Note that by independence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\langle\gamma_{0},\gamma_{i}^{*}\rangle)=0,

and that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}(\langle\gamma_{0},\gamma_{i}-\gamma_{i}^{*}\rangle)\big|=\big|\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbb{I}_{\{\gamma_{i}\neq\gamma_{i}^{*}\}}\langle\gamma_{0},\gamma_{i}-\gamma_{i}^{*}\rangle\big)\big|\leq\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbb{I}_{\{\gamma_{i}\neq\gamma_{i}^{*}\}}\|\gamma_{0}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\|\gamma_{i}-\gamma_{i}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbb{I}_{\{\gamma_{i}\neq\gamma_{i}^{*}\}}\|\gamma_{0}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\big)\leq 2\mathbb{P}(\gamma_{i}^{*}\not=\gamma_{i})^{1/r}\mathbb{E}(\|\gamma_{0}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q})^{1/q}\leq 2\beta^{1/r}(i)\mathbb{E}(\|\gamma_{0}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q})^{1/q},

where the second inequality follows by Triangle inequality and the fact that
\delta_{i}
and
\delta_{i}^{*}
have the same distribution, the third inequality follows by Hölder’s inequality and that
\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma_{i}
have the same distribution.
∎

Lemma 29
.

Suppose that Assumption 1 holds. Let
r,q>1
be such that
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}=1
. In particular, suppose
\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\beta^{1/r}(l)<\infty
and
\sup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left(\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}\right)<\infty
as stated by Assumption 1.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\right)\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bigl\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr)=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl\|\gamma_{i}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\bigr\}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bigl\langle\gamma_{i},\gamma_{j}\bigr\rangle\bigr\}.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}
= 1, so that
\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\|\gamma_{i}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\right\}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}
. In addition,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\gamma_{i},\gamma_{j}\right\rangle\right\}\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=i+1}^{\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left\{\left\langle\gamma_{i},\gamma_{j}\right\rangle\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=i+1}^{\infty}\beta^{1/r}(j-i)\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bigl\|\gamma_{i}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}\bigr)^{1/q}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl(\bigl\|\gamma_{i}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}\bigr)^{1/q}\lesssim\frac{1}{n},

where the second inequality follows from
Lemma

28
and the third inequality by the fact that from Assumption 1 we have
\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\beta^{1/r}(l)<\infty
.
∎

S10
Examples of spatial processes satisfying Assumption 2

In this section, we provide concrete examples of stochastic processes

\{\gamma_{i}(\cdot)\}_{i=1}^{n}
satisfying Assumption 2
b
–
c
. Throughout, all processes are assumed to be measurable on
[0,1]^{d}
.

S10.1
Gaussian processes

Gaussian processes are a standard model for spatial and spatio-temporal variability; see, e.g.,
Rasmussen and Williams (2006)
;
Stein (1999)
.

Proposition 30
.

Suppose that
\{\gamma_{i}(\cdot)\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. mean-zero Gaussian processes on
[0,1]^{d}
with covariance function
K
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}K(x,x)\leq C<\infty.

Then Assumption 2
b
–
c
holds.

Proof.

Fix
i
and
x\in[0,1]^{d}
. Then

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}(x)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{x}^{2}),\qquad\sigma_{x}^{2}=K(x,x)\leq C.

Hence, for any
c\in(0,(2C)^{-1})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp\{c\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}\}=(1-2c\sigma_{x}^{2})^{-1/2}\leq(1-2cC)^{-1/2}<\infty.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}\exp\{c\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}\}<\infty,

which verifies Assumption 2
b
.

Next, let
q>1
. Since
[0,1]^{d}
has Lebesgue measure one, Jensen’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}=\left(\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}\,dx\right)^{q}\leq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\gamma_{i}(x)|^{2q}\,dx.

Taking expectations and using Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}\leq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}|\gamma_{i}(x)|^{2q}\,dx.

For a centered Gaussian random variable
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|Z|^{2q}=\sigma^{2q}\mathbb{E}|N(0,1)|^{2q}.

Thus

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}|\gamma_{i}(x)|^{2q}\leq C^{q}\mathbb{E}|N(0,1)|^{2q}<\infty,

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}<\infty.

This verifies Assumption 2
c
.
∎

S10.2
Bounded stochastic processes

Bounded stochastic processes naturally arise in applications where the underlying spatial signal is constrained. Examples include normalized measurements, physical quantities with known bounds, or truncated models; see, for example,
Cressie (1993)
.

Proposition 31
.

Suppose that
\{\gamma_{i}(\cdot)\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mean-zero stochastic processes satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}|\gamma_{i}(x)|\leq C\qquad\text{almost surely}

for some constant
C>0
. Then Assumption 2
b
–
c
holds.

Proof.

The mean-zero condition ensures that
\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{i}(x)]=0
for all
x
, as required in Assumption 2
b
.

For every
x\in[0,1]^{d}
,

\displaystyle e^{c\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}}\leq e^{cC^{2}}\qquad\text{almost surely}.

Hence

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}e^{c\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}}\leq e^{cC^{2}}<\infty,

so Assumption 2
b
holds.

Also,

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}\,dx\leq C^{2}

almost surely. Therefore, for every
q>1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}\leq C^{2q}<\infty.

Thus Assumption 2
c
holds.
∎

S10.3
Processes with uniformly sub-Gaussian marginals

Processes with sub-exponential tails arise in models with moderate tail behavior. They are commonly used in high-dimensional probability and statistical learning; see, for example,
Vershynin (2018)
.

Proposition 32
.

Suppose that
\{\gamma_{i}(\cdot)\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mean-zero processes and there exists
\nu>0
such that for every
x\in[0,1]^{d}
and every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{t\gamma_{i}(x)}\leq e^{\nu^{2}t^{2}/2}.

Then Assumption 2
b
holds. If, in addition,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}|\gamma_{i}(x)|^{2q}<\infty

for some
q>1
, then Assumption 2
c
also holds.

Proof.

A standard fact for sub-Gaussian random variables implies that there exists a constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(\nu)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{c_{0}\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}}\leq 2\qquad\text{for all }x\in[0,1]^{d}.

Hence

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}e^{c_{0}\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}}<\infty,

which proves Assumption 2
b
.

If moreover
\sup_{x}\mathbb{E}|\gamma_{i}(x)|^{2q}<\infty
, then by Jensen and Fubini, as in the proof of Proposition
30
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}\leq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}|\gamma_{i}(x)|^{2q}\,dx\leq\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}|\gamma_{i}(x)|^{2q}<\infty.

Thus Assumption 2
c
holds.
∎

S10.4
Random basis expansion processes with Rademacher coefficients

Random basis expansions are widely used in functional data analysis and spatial statistics; see, e.g.,
Ramsay and Silverman (2005)
;
Ferraty and Vieu (2006)
.

Proposition 33
.

Suppose that

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\,\varepsilon_{ik}\,\phi_{k}(x),\qquad x\in[0,1]^{d},

where:

1.

\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{K}
are deterministic bounded functions on
[0,1]^{d}
;

2.

a_{1},\dots,a_{K}\in\mathbb{R}
are deterministic constants;

3.

\varepsilon_{i1},\dots,\varepsilon_{iK}
are independent Rademacher random variables, that is,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon_{ik}=1)=\mathbb{P}(\varepsilon_{ik}=-1)=\frac{1}{2}.

Then Assumption 2
b
–
c
holds.

Proof.

For each fixed
x\in[0,1]^{d}
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}\phi_{k}(x)\varepsilon_{ik}.

Since
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{ik}]=0
and the sum is finite,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{i}(x)]=0,

so the mean-zero condition in Assumption 2
b
holds.

Moreover, since the
\varepsilon_{ik}
’s are bounded and independent, Hoeffding’s lemma implies that for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp\{ta_{k}\phi_{k}(x)\varepsilon_{ik}\}\leq\exp\!\left(\frac{t^{2}a_{k}^{2}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}}{2}\right).

Using independence across
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{t\gamma_{i}(x)}\leq\exp\!\left(\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}^{2}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}\right).

Define

\displaystyle V:=\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}^{2}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}<\infty.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{t\gamma_{i}(x)}\leq e^{Vt^{2}/2},

so
\gamma_{i}(x)
is uniformly sub-Gaussian. Therefore, there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x}\mathbb{E}e^{c\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}}<\infty,

which verifies Assumption 2
b
.
∎

S10.5
Karhunen–Loève type expansion models with bounded coefficients

The Karhunen–Loève expansion is a classical representation of stochastic processes and also underlies functional principal component analysis; see, e.g.,
Bosq (2000)
;
Ash and Gardner (1975)
. The following proposition gives a non-Gaussian version of this construction.

Proposition 34
.

Suppose that

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\,\eta_{ik}\,\phi_{k}(x),\qquad x\in[0,1]^{d},

where:

1.

\{\phi_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
is an orthonormal basis of
L_{2}([0,1]^{d})
;

2.

\lambda_{k}\geq 0
and

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}<\infty;

3.

\{\eta_{ik}\}_{k\geq 1}
are independent mean-zero random variables satisfying

\displaystyle|\eta_{ik}|\leq C_{0}\qquad\text{almost surely}

for some constant
C_{0}>0
.

Then Assumption 2
b
–
c
holds.

Proof.

Fix
x\in[0,1]^{d}
. For each
m\geq 1
, define the partial sums

\displaystyle S_{m}(x):=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\,\eta_{ik}\,\phi_{k}(x).

Step 1: Well-definedness.

Let
M<L
. Since
\{\eta_{ik}\}
are independent, mean-zero, and bounded by
C_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(S_{L}(x)-S_{M}(x)\bigr)^{2}=\sum_{k=M+1}^{L}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}\mathbb{E}(\eta_{ik}^{2})\leq C_{0}^{2}\sum_{k=M+1}^{L}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}.

By assumption (2), the series
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}
converges uniformly in
x
, hence the right-hand side tends to zero as
m,\ell\to\infty
. Thus
S_{m}(x)
is Cauchy in
L_{2}
, and therefore converges in
L_{2}
(and along a subsequence almost surely) to a limit, which we denote by
\gamma_{i}(x)
.

Step 2: Sub-Gaussian bound.

By Hoeffding’s lemma, for each
k
and every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp\{t\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\phi_{k}(x)\eta_{ik}\}\leq\exp\!\left(\frac{t^{2}C_{0}^{2}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}}{2}\right).

Using independence, we obtain for every
m\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{tS_{m}(x)}=\prod_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\exp\{t\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\phi_{k}(x)\eta_{ik}\}\leq\exp\!\bigl(\frac{t^{2}C_{0}^{2}}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}\bigr).

Define

\displaystyle V:=\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}<\infty.

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{tS_{m}(x)}\leq\exp\!\left(\frac{t^{2}C_{0}^{2}V}{2}\right)\qquad\text{for all }m.

Passing to the limit and using Fatou’s lemma,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{t\gamma_{i}(x)}\leq\liminf_{m\to\infty}\mathbb{E}e^{tS_{m}(x)}\leq\exp\!\left(\frac{t^{2}C_{0}^{2}V}{2}\right).

Hence
\gamma_{i}(x)
is uniformly sub-Gaussian in
x
. Consequently, there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathbb{E}e^{c\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}}<\infty,

which verifies Assumption 2
b
.

Step 3:
L_{2}
-moment bound.

Since
\{\phi_{k}\}
is orthonormal in
L_{2}([0,1]^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\gamma_{i}(x)^{2}\,dx=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\eta_{ik}^{2}

almost surely.

Using
|\eta_{ik}|\leq C_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq C_{0}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}.

Moreover, by Tonelli’s theorem,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(x)^{2}\,dx\leq V<\infty.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}\leq\left(C_{0}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}\right)^{q}<\infty\quad\text{almost surely},

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2q}<\infty\qquad\text{for every }q>1.

This verifies Assumption 2
c
.
∎

S11
General
Function-Class
Bound

In this section, we state and prove an extension of Theorem 1 in the Main Manuscript to
general estimation classes
. The proof of Theorem 1 only uses two ingredients: an approximation bound by a function in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{F}_{nm}}
, and a covering number bound for the corresponding truncated class. We therefore formulate the result in terms of an abstract entropy rate.

Empirical norms.

For any index set
I\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
, define

\displaystyle\|g\|_{I,m}^{2}:=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\frac{1}{m_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}g^{2}(x_{ij}).

We use the shorthand

\displaystyle\|g\|_{nm}^{2}:=\|g\|_{\{1,\dots,n\},m}^{2},\quad\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}^{2}:=\|g\|_{\mathcal{J}_{e,s},m}^{2},\quad\|g\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}^{2}:=\|g\|_{\mathcal{J}_{o,s},m}^{2}.

Theorem 35

(General
function-class
bound)
.

Suppose that Assumptions 1–4 hold
, except for Assumption 3
c
.

For each
n
and
m
, let
\mathfrak{F}_{nm}
be a nonempty deterministic pointwise measurable class of measurable functions from
[0,1]^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}
.
Let
\widehat{f}
be
a measurable minimizer
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{F}_{nm}}

of the empirical criterion
in (2) in the Main Manuscript. Assume that there exists
a deterministic

\overline{f}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{F}_{nm}}
such that

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\qquad\|\overline{f}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{A}_{nm}}

(61)

for some sequence
\varepsilon_{nm}>0
.

Write
\mathfrak{F}_{nm,\mathcal{A}_{nm}}:=\{f_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}:f\in\mathfrak{F}_{nm}\}
.

Let
(S_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be any sequence satisfying

\displaystyle C_{\beta}\log(n)\leq S_{n}\leq n,

for a sufficiently large constant
C_{\beta}>0
depending only on the mixing rate in Assumption 4.

(Blockwise entropy condition).

Suppose there exists a sequence
(\rho_{n})_{n\geq 1}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\leq 1}\;\max_{0\leq s\leq S_{n}-1}\max\!\bigl\{\sup_{z}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathfrak{F}_{nm,\mathcal{A}_{nm}},\|\cdot\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m})}{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m\,\log(\delta^{-1})},

\displaystyle\quad\;\sup_{z}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathfrak{F}_{nm,\mathcal{A}_{nm}},\|\cdot\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m})}{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m\,\log(\delta^{-1})}\bigr\}\leq C\rho_{n}.

(62)

(Global entropy condition).

Suppose there exists a sequence
(\widetilde{\rho}_{n})_{n\geq 1}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\leq 1}\;\sup_{z_{1},\dots,z_{nm}}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}\!\left(\delta,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{F}_{nm,\mathcal{A}_{nm}}},\|\cdot\|_{nm}\right)}{nm\,\log(\delta^{-1})}\leq C\widetilde{\rho}_{n}.

(63)

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}\asymp\max\{\sigma_{\epsilon},\sigma_{\gamma}\}\sqrt{\log(nm)},

and
(a_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be any sequence such that
a_{n}\to\infty
. Then, with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(64)

Suppose moreover that
Assumption 3
c
holds,
\mathfrak{F}_{nm}=\mathcal{F}(L_{nm},r_{nm})
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(L_{nm},r_{nm})}
are selected as in Lemma
1
. Then we may take

\displaystyle\rho_{n}=\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{4.3}(nm),\qquad\widetilde{\rho}_{n}=\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Substituting into (
64
) yields

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{5.3}(nm)

(65)

\displaystyle+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

Choosing
S_{n}\asymp\log n
, and using
\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\gtrsim\phi_{nm}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{5.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(66)

Under the architecture choice in Lemma
1
,
\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\phi_{nm}
, and therefore

\displaystyle a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{5.3}(nm)\lesssim a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}+1)\phi_{nm}\log^{7.3}(nm),

which recovers the rate in Theorem 1.

Proof.

The proof follows the same line of argument as the proof of Theorem 1 in Appendix
S2.2
. The only difference is that we now distinguish between the entropy input used in the blockwise arguments and the entropy input used in the global argument.

Let

\displaystyle\Delta_{nm}(x):=\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x)-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x).

Also define the shifted truncated class

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f}):=\bigl\{f_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}:\,f\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{F}_{nm}}\bigr\}.

For every
0<\delta\leq 1
, every
z_{1},\dots,z_{nm}\in[0,1]^{d}
, and every
h_{1},h_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{F}_{nm,\mathcal{A}_{nm}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(h_{1}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}})-(h_{2}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}})\|_{nm}=\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{nm}.

Likewise, for every block
\mathcal{J}_{e,s}
and
\mathcal{J}_{o,s}
,

\displaystyle\|(h_{1}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}})-(h_{2}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}})\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m}=\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m},

and

\displaystyle\|(h_{1}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}})-(h_{2}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}})\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}=\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m}.

Hence translation by
-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
is an isometry from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathfrak{F}_{nm,\mathcal{A}_{nm}}}
onto
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
in the global empirical norm and in each block empirical norm. Therefore, by the blockwise entropy condition and the global entropy condition, the same bounds hold for
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
with the same constants
C\rho_{n}
and
C\widetilde{\rho}_{n}
.

Define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=\bigl\{|\epsilon_{ij}|\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\,\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n,\,1\leq j\leq m_{i},\quad\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\infty,nm}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\,\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n\bigr\}.

By Lemma
2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})=o(1).

By the assumption
\|\overline{f}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{A}_{nm}
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{f}=\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}.

(67)

Thus it suffices to bound
\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}
.

On the event
\mathcal{E}
, exactly as in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem 1, the empirical basic inequality gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}(x_{ij})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})+\epsilon_{ij}\bigr\}.

(68)

We first control the approximation term. By Cauchy–Schwarz and
(
61
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}(x_{ij})\bigr\}\leq\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\,\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{nm}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\,\varepsilon_{nm}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}.

(69)

Step 1: the
\epsilon
-term.

Since
\Delta_{nm}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, the blockwise entropy condition implies that the proof of

Corollary

19

can be repeated block by block for the shifted class
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, with the quantity
\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{\cdot,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)
replaced by
\rho_{n}
. Thus, arguing exactly as in Step 3 of the proof of Theorem 1, we obtain that with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq C\,\sigma_{\epsilon}\,\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}(\Delta_{nm}),

(70)

where
\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}
is defined exactly as
\Lambda_{n,S}
in

Corollary

19
, but with
\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{\cdot,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)
replaced by
\rho_{n}
. Repeating the same Jensen reduction as in the proof of Theorem 1 gives

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}(\Delta_{nm})\leq C\,\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\,\log\!\left(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\right)}.

(71)

Applying Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr).

(72)

Since
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}
, this becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr).

(73)

Step 2: norm comparison.

Using again the blockwise entropy condition, the proofs of

Corollary

10

and

Lemma

20

carry over block by block with
\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{5.3}(nm/S_{n})
replaced by
\rho_{n}\log(nm)
. Therefore, for any sequence
a_{n}\to\infty
, arguing exactly as in Step 4 of the proof of Theorem 1, we obtain with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+Ca_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}.

(74)

Step 3: the
\gamma
-term.

We decompose the
\gamma
-term exactly as in the proof of Theorem 1:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})

\displaystyle={}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}.

For the second term, by Lemma
29
and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}.

(75)

Combining this with (
74
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\leq\frac{8\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{1}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}.

(76)

For the centered fluctuation term, we now use the global entropy condition. Since
\Delta_{nm}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, the proof of

Corollary

13

carries over verbatim with
\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)
replaced by
\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)
. Hence, with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C\,\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\,\log(nm).

(77)

Using (
74
) once more, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{2}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}+C_{3}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm).

(78)

Combining (
76
) and
(
78
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\leq\frac{3}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{4}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}+C_{5}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+C_{6}\,\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(79)

Step 4: empirical norm bound.

Substituting (
69
),
(
73
), and
(
79
) into
(
68
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(80)

We now control the logarithmic factor exactly as in the proof of Theorem 1. If

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n},

then (
80
) already yields the desired conclusion. Otherwise,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}>\frac{1}{n},\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\geq n^{-1/2},

so that

\displaystyle\log\!\left(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\right)\leq\log\!\left(2\mathcal{A}_{nm}\sqrt{n}\right)\lesssim\log(nm).

Therefore, in this case,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(81)

Since
a_{n}\to\infty
, we have
a_{n}\geq 1
for all sufficiently large
n
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)\leq a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm).

Hence in either case,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(82)

Step 5: passage to
\mathcal{L}_{2}
.

Combining (
74
) and
(
82
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+Ca_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(83)

Substituting (
83
) into
(
67
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

with probability tending to one. This proves
(
64
).

To recover Theorem 1,
take
\mathfrak{F}_{nm}=\mathcal{F}(L_{nm},r_{nm})
under Assumption 3
c
in the Main Manuscript and
choose
(L_{nm},r_{nm})
according to either architecture in Lemma
1
and set
S_{n}\asymp\log n
, with a sufficiently large proportionality constant. Lemma
1
gives

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\phi_{nm}.

The covering-number bound in Lemma
24
, which is uniform over the design points, has numerator of order

\displaystyle nm\phi_{nm}\log^{3.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1}).

Since every even or odd block contains order
nm/S_{n}
observations, the blockwise and global entropy conditions hold with

\displaystyle\rho_{n}\lesssim S_{n}\phi_{nm}\log^{3.3}(nm)\lesssim\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{4.3}(nm),\qquad\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\lesssim\phi_{nm}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Substituting these bounds into (
64
) gives

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;\phi_{nm}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{5.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)

\displaystyle+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

Because
\phi_{nm}\leq\phi_{nm/S_{n}}
, the global entropy term is dominated by the blockwise term. Moreover, the choice of
S_{n}
gives
\beta(S_{n})\lesssim n^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\leq S_{n}\phi_{nm}\lesssim\phi_{nm}\log(nm).

Together with

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\lesssim(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm),

these bounds yield
