\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}+1)\phi_{nm}\log^{7.3}(nm)+a_{n}\frac{(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm)}{n},

which is the bound in Theorem 1.

∎

S11.1
Architecture-Dependent Risk Bound

We next restate and prove the architecture-dependent corollary given
in the Main Manuscript.

Corollary 36

(Architecture-dependent risk bound)
.

Retain the hypotheses and notation of
Theorem
35
, except for
the blockwise and global entropy conditions
(
62
) and
(
63
). Define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{nm}:=L_{nm}^{2}r_{nm}^{2}\log\!\left(eL_{nm}r_{nm}^{2}\right).

(84)

Then the entropy conditions
(
62
) and
(
63
)
are satisfied with

\displaystyle\rho_{n}\lesssim\frac{S_{n}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{1.3}(nm),\qquad\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{1.3}(nm).

(85)

Consequently, with probability approaching one,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\frac{S_{n}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{2.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(86)

Proof.

Fix deterministic
L_{nm},r_{nm}\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}
and take

\mathfrak{F}_{nm}=\mathcal{F}(L_{nm},r_{nm})
.
The estimator and the deterministic comparator are those in the
hypotheses of the corollary. For all sufficiently large
n
, we have

\mathcal{A}_{nm}\geq 1
and
nm\geq e
.

Step 1: the network class and its covering numbers.

A network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{nm},r_{nm})
is determined by

\displaystyle W_{nm}=(d+1)r_{nm}+(L_{nm}-1)r_{nm}(r_{nm}+1)+(r_{nm}+1)

weights and biases. Write
f_{\theta}
for the network corresponding
to
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{W_{nm}}
.
For every
x\in[0,1]^{d}
, the map
\theta\mapsto f_{\theta}(x)

is continuous, since it is obtained by finitely many affine maps
and ReLU operations.
For any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{W_{nm}}
, choose

\theta_{k}\in\mathbb{Q}^{W_{nm}}
with
\theta_{k}\to\theta
.
Then
f_{\theta_{k}}(x)\to f_{\theta}(x)
for every
x
.
Thus the countable subclass

\{f_{\theta}:\theta\in\mathbb{Q}^{W_{nm}}\}
establishes pointwise
measurability. The zero network belongs to the class, and truncation
preserves pointwise convergence.

Only the first bound in Lemma
24

is used below; the restrictions in (
6
) are not imposed.
To include
L_{nm}=r_{nm}=1
, put

r_{nm}^{+}=\max\{r_{nm},2\}
.
Adding neurons whose incoming and outgoing weights are zero gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(L_{nm},r_{nm})\subseteq\mathcal{F}(L_{nm},r_{nm}^{+}).

Moreover, since
r_{nm}^{+}\leq 2r_{nm}
,

\displaystyle L_{nm}^{2}(r_{nm}^{+})^{2}\log\!\bigl(L_{nm}(r_{nm}^{+})^{2}\bigr)\leq 4L_{nm}^{2}r_{nm}^{2}\log(4L_{nm}r_{nm}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq 4(1+\log 4)\,L_{nm}^{2}r_{nm}^{2}\log(eL_{nm}r_{nm}^{2})

\displaystyle=4(1+\log 4)\mathfrak{V}_{nm}.

A
(\delta/2)
-cover of the larger truncated class

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm}^{+})

yields a
\delta
-cover of the smaller class

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm})
:
from each covering ball that intersects

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm})
,
select one point of that intersection.
The triangle inequality bounds the distance to the selected point
by
\delta
. Thus applying the first bound of
Lemma
24
to the
padded class

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm}^{+})
,
at radius
\delta/2
, gives the following bound
(
87
) also
when
L_{nm}=r_{nm}=1
.

The covering-number bound in
Lemma
24
is uniform over the design
points. Thus, for any empirical norm based on
N
design points and
every
0<\delta\leq 1/2
,

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{1},\ldots,z_{N}\in[0,1]^{d}}\log\mathcal{N}\!\bigl(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm}),\|\cdot\|_{N}\bigr)\lesssim\mathfrak{V}_{nm}\log\!\bigl(e\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}\bigr),

(87)

where

\|g\|_{N}^{2}:=N^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{N}g^{2}(z_{k})
.

Step 2: the weighted empirical norms.

Let
c_{m},C_{m}>0
satisfy
c_{m}m\leq m_{i}\leq C_{m}m
,
and set
\kappa=(C_{m}/c_{m})^{1/2}\geq 1
.
Fix a nonempty
I\subseteq[n]
and a deterministic array

z=(z_{ij}:i\in I,\ j\in[m_{i}])
.
Define

\displaystyle M_{I}:=\sum_{i\in I}m_{i},\qquad\|g\|_{M_{I},z}^{2}:=\frac{1}{M_{I}}\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}g(z_{ij})^{2},

and

\displaystyle\|g\|_{I,m,z}^{2}:=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\frac{1}{m_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}g(z_{ij})^{2}.

Since
M_{I}\leq C_{m}|I|m
, for every
g
,

\displaystyle\|g\|_{I,m,z}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c_{m}|I|m}\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}g(z_{ij})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{I}}{c_{m}|I|m}\|g\|_{M_{I},z}^{2}\leq\kappa^{2}\|g\|_{M_{I},z}^{2}.

(88)

The same inequality applies to differences of functions.
A
(\delta/\kappa)
-cover in
\|\cdot\|_{M_{I},z}
is therefore
a
\delta
-cover in
\|\cdot\|_{I,m,z}
.
Applying (
87
) with

N=M_{I}
and radius
\delta/\kappa
, we obtain, for some
C_{1}>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{z}\log\mathcal{N}\!\left(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm}),\|\cdot\|_{I,m,z}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{1}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}\log\!\left(e\kappa\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}\right),\qquad 0<\delta\leq 1/2.

(89)

The constant
C_{1}
is independent of
I
and
z
.

Choose
C_{A}\geq 1
such that

\mathcal{A}_{nm}\leq C_{A}\sqrt{\log(nm)}
for all sufficiently
large
n
.
For
0<\delta\leq 1/2
, we have
\log(\delta^{-1})\geq\log 2
,
and hence

\displaystyle\frac{\log(e\kappa\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1})}{\log(\delta^{-1})}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\log(e\kappa\mathcal{A}_{nm})}{\log(\delta^{-1})}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{1+\log(\kappa C_{A})+\tfrac{1}{2}\log\log(nm)}{\log 2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\log^{1.3}(nm),

where one may take

\displaystyle C_{2}=1+\frac{3/2+\log(\kappa C_{A})}{\log 2}.

The last inequality uses
\log(nm)\geq 1
and

\log\log(nm)\leq\log(nm)\leq\log^{1.3}(nm)
.
In particular,

\displaystyle\log\!\bigl(e\mathcal{A}_{nm}\delta^{-1}\bigr)\lesssim\log^{1.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1}),\qquad 0<\delta\leq 1/2.

(90)

Combining the weighted covering bound with the preceding calculation,
and writing
C_{3}=C_{1}C_{2}
, gives

\displaystyle\sup_{z}\log\mathcal{N}\!\left(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm}),\|\cdot\|_{I,m,z}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{3}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}\log^{1.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1}),\qquad 0<\delta\leq 1/2,

(91)

uniformly over all nonempty
I\subseteq[n]
.

Step 3: the blockwise and global entropy rates.

Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{J}_{n}:=\{\mathcal{J}_{e,s},\mathcal{J}_{o,s}:0\leq s<S_{n}\}\setminus\{\varnothing\},\qquad K_{n}=\lfloor n/S_{n}\rfloor.

For
K_{n}\geq 2
, the block construction and
(
33
)
give, for every
J\in\mathfrak{J}_{n}
,

\displaystyle|J|\geq\frac{K_{n}-1}{2}\geq\frac{K_{n}+1}{6}>\frac{n}{6S_{n}}.

If
K_{n}=1
, every nonempty block satisfies
|J|\geq 1
, whereas

n/S_{n}<2
; thus
|J|\geq n/(6S_{n})
in this case as well.
Also,

\displaystyle|J|\leq\frac{K_{n}+2}{2}\leq\frac{n/S_{n}+2}{2}\leq\frac{3n}{2S_{n}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\frac{n}{6S_{n}}\leq|J|\leq\frac{3n}{2S_{n}},\qquad J\in\mathfrak{J}_{n}.

(92)

For all sufficiently large
n
, define

\displaystyle\rho_{n}:=6C_{3}\frac{S_{n}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{1.3}(nm),\widetilde{\rho}_{n}:=C_{3}\frac{\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{1.3}(nm).

(93)

At the finitely many remaining indices, set both sequences equal
to one. The resulting sequences are positive and deterministic.
For every
J\in\mathfrak{J}_{n}
and
0<\delta\leq 1/2
,
(
91
) and
(
92
) imply

\displaystyle\sup_{z}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}\!\left(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm}),\|\cdot\|_{J,m,z}\right)}{|J|m\log(\delta^{-1})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{3}\frac{\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{|J|m}\log^{1.3}(nm)\leq 6C_{3}\frac{S_{n}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{1.3}(nm)=\rho_{n}.

Taking the supremum over
0<\delta\leq 1/2
and the maximum
over the nonempty even and odd blocks verifies both blockwise
entropy conditions in (
62
).

For
I=[n]
, the same weighted covering bound gives

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\leq 1/2}\sup_{z}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}\!\left(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L_{nm},r_{nm}),\|\cdot\|_{[n],m,z}\right)}{nm\log(\delta^{-1})}\leq C_{3}\frac{\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{1.3}(nm)=\widetilde{\rho}_{n}.

This verifies the global entropy condition.

This proves (
85
).

Step 4: application of the general function-class bound.

Substituting these entropy rates into
(
64
) gives
with probability approaching one

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;\varepsilon_{nm}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\frac{S_{n}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{2.3}(nm)

\displaystyle+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{2.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(94)

Because
S_{n}\geq 1
, the global entropy contribution is bounded by
the blockwise contribution.

Indeed,

\displaystyle a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\frac{(S_{n}+1)\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{2.3}(nm)\leq 2a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\frac{S_{n}\mathfrak{V}_{nm}}{nm}\log^{2.3}(nm).

Therefore,
(
86
) follows.
∎

S12
Approximate Manifold Support under Temporal-Spatial Dependence

In this section, we extend the temporal–spatial risk bound to the case where the design is supported on a tubular neighborhood of a lower-dimensional manifold. The argument is an application of Theorem
35
: once an approximation bound is established on the support of the design, the temporal–spatial empirical-process and dependence terms are handled exactly as in the general architecture regime.

The only new ingredient is the approximation lemma below, which adapts the geometric approximation step of Theorem 6.1 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
to the present setting. Their argument combines three ingredients: a Johnson–Lindenstrauss-type dimension reduction for the manifold, approximation of the reduced function via Theorem 3.3 and Corollary 3.1, and a transfer step from the manifold to its
\rho
-neighborhood. See Theorem 6.1 and its proof in the supplementary material of
Jiao et al. (2023)
for details.

Lemma 37

(Approximation under approximate manifold support)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\subset[0,1]^{d}
be a compact
d_{M}
-dimensional Riemannian manifold and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\tau}:=\left\{x\in[0,1]^{d}:\inf_{y\in\mathcal{M}}\|x-y\|\leq\tau\right\}.

Assume that
f^{*}:[0,1]^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is
(p,C)
-smooth, with
p=q+s
,
q\in\mathbf{N}
,
s\in(0,1]
, and that all partial derivatives of order at most
q
are uniformly bounded.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
. Let
d_{\delta}\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}
satisfy

\displaystyle d_{M}\leq d_{\delta}<d,\qquad d_{\delta}\lesssim\frac{d_{M}\log(d/\delta)}{\delta^{2}}.

Set

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}:=(nm)^{-\frac{p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}.

Assume that
\rho
satisfies

\displaystyle\tau\leq c_{p,C,d,d_{M},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{\,1/(p\wedge 1)}.

(95)

Then there exists
\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
, with either

\displaystyle L\asymp\log(nm),\qquad r\asymp(nm)^{\frac{d_{\delta}}{2(2p+d_{\delta})}},

or

\displaystyle L\asymp\log(nm)\,(nm)^{\frac{d_{\delta}}{2(2p+d_{\delta})}},\qquad r=O(1),

such that

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}_{\tau}}^{2}\leq C_{p,C,d,d_{M},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2},

(96)

where

\|f\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}_{\tau}}:=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{M}_{\tau}}|f(x)|
. The constants
c_{p,C,d,d_{M},\delta}
and
C_{p,C,d,d_{M},\delta}
do not depend on
n
,
m
, or
\tau
.

Proof.

The proof follows a projection–approximation–extension scheme.

Step 1: Dimension reduction on
\mathcal{M}
.

By the dimension-reduction construction in the proof of Theorem 6.1 of

Jiao et al. (2023)
(Appendix A.7 of the supplementary material), there exists
a linear map

\displaystyle A:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\delta}},\qquad d_{\delta}\lesssim\frac{d_{M}\log(d/\delta)}{\delta^{2}},

such that

\displaystyle AA^{\top}=\frac{d}{d_{\delta}}I_{d_{\delta}}

and

\displaystyle(1-\delta)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\leq\|Ax_{1}-Ax_{2}\|\leq(1+\delta)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,\qquad x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{M}.

In particular, the restriction of
A
to
\mathcal{M}
is injective.

Moreover, since
\mathcal{M}_{\tau}\subset[0,1]^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle A(\mathcal{M}_{\tau})\subset A([0,1]^{d})\subset E:=\Bigl[-\sqrt{d/d_{\delta}},\,\sqrt{d/d_{\delta}}\Bigr]^{d_{\delta}},

exactly as in Appendix A.7 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
.

For each
z\in A(\mathcal{M})
, there exists a unique
x_{z}\in\mathcal{M}
such that

Ax_{z}=z
. Indeed, if
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}
satisfy
Ax=Ax^{\prime}=z
, then

\displaystyle(1-\delta)\|x-x^{\prime}\|\leq\|Ax-Ax^{\prime}\|=0,

which implies
x=x^{\prime}
.

We therefore define a map

\displaystyle T:A(\mathcal{M})\to\mathcal{M},\qquad T(z):=x_{z}.

By the construction in Appendix A.7 (pp. 9–10) of the supplementary material of
Jiao et al. (2023)
, the map
T
is differentiable on
A(\mathcal{M})
, and the norm of its derivative lies in

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{1+\delta},\frac{1}{1-\delta}\right].

Define the reduced function

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}:A(\mathcal{M})\to\mathbb{R},\qquad\widetilde{f}(z):=f^{*}(T(z)).

Since
f^{*}
is
(p,C)
-smooth on
[0,1]^{d}
, it follows, as in Appendix A.7
of
Jiao et al. (2023)
, that

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}\text{ is }(p,\,C(1-\delta)^{-p})\text{-smooth on }A(\mathcal{M}).

Since
\mathcal{M}
is compact and
A
is continuous, the set
A(\mathcal{M})
is compact,
hence closed in
\mathbb{R}^{d_{\delta}}
. Moreover,
A(\mathcal{M})\subset E
.
Therefore, by the extended version of Whitney’s extension theorem in

Fefferman (2006)
, there exists a function

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}:E\to\mathbb{R}

such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}(z)=\widetilde{f}(z),\qquad z\in A(\mathcal{M}),

and
\widetilde{F}
is
(p,C_{\mathrm{ext}}\,C(1-\delta)^{-p})
-smooth on
E
,
where
C_{\mathrm{ext}}>0
depends only on
p
and
d_{\delta}
.

Step 2: Approximation in reduced dimension.

Let
\Phi:E\to[0,1]^{d_{\delta}}
be an affine bijection. Since
\widetilde{F}
is
(p,C_{\mathrm{ext}}C(1-\delta)^{-p})
-smooth on
E
, it follows that
\widetilde{F}\circ\Phi^{-1}
is
(p,\widetilde{C}_{p,C_{\mathrm{ext}},C,d,d_{\delta},\delta})
-smooth on
[0,1]^{d_{\delta}}
. Therefore, by Corollary 3.1 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
, there exists a ReLU network
h_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{F}\circ\Phi^{-1}-h_{0}\|_{\infty,[0,1]^{d_{\delta}}}\leq C_{p,C_{\mathrm{ext}},C,d,d_{\delta},\delta}\,(nm)^{-\frac{p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}.

Defining
h:=h_{0}\circ\Phi
, we have for every
x\in E
,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{F}-h)(x)=(\widetilde{F}\circ\Phi^{-1})(\Phi(x))-h_{0}(\Phi(x)).

Since
\Phi
is a bijection from
E
onto
[0,1]^{d_{\delta}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{F}-h\|_{\infty,E}=\|\widetilde{F}\circ\Phi^{-1}-h_{0}\|_{\infty,[0,1]^{d_{\delta}}}\leq C_{p,C_{\mathrm{ext}},C,d,d_{\delta},\delta}\,(nm)^{-\frac{p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}-h\|_{\infty,A(\mathcal{M})}\leq C_{p,C_{\mathrm{ext}},C,d,d_{\delta},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Define

\displaystyle\overline{f}(x):=h(Ax),\qquad x\in[0,1]^{d}.

Since
A
is linear, the mapping
x\mapsto Ax
can be absorbed into the first
layer of the network, and hence
\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)

with the same order of depth and width.
In fact, observe that if
h\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
, then by Definition 1 in the Main Manuscript,

\displaystyle h(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{1,i}^{(L)}f_{i}^{(L)}(z)+c_{1,0}^{(L)},

where the functions
f_{i}^{(s)}

satisfy the recursive relations in Equations (4)–(5), in the Main Manuscript. For the first layer,

\displaystyle f_{i}^{(1)}(Ax)

\displaystyle=\rho\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\delta}}c_{i,j}^{(0)}(Ax)^{(j)}+c_{i,0}^{(0)}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\delta}}c_{i,j}^{(0)}A_{j,k}\right)x^{(k)}+c_{i,0}^{(0)}\right).

Thus, after redefining the first-layer coefficients by

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}_{i,k}^{(0)}:=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\delta}}c_{i,j}^{(0)}A_{j,k},

the composition
x\mapsto h(Ax)
has the same recursive form as Equations (4)–(5), in the Main Manuscript, with the same number of layers
L
and the same width
r
.
Hence

\displaystyle\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r).

Step 3: Extension to the tubular neighborhood.

Let
x\in\mathcal{M}_{\tau}
. Then, by definition of
\mathcal{M}_{\tau}
, there exists

y_{x}\in\mathcal{M}
such that

\displaystyle\|x-y_{x}\|\leq\tau.

Since
\overline{f}(x)=h(Ax)
, we may write

\displaystyle f^{*}(x)-\overline{f}(x)=\bigl(f^{*}(x)-f^{*}(y_{x})\bigr)+\bigl(f^{*}(y_{x})-h(Ax)\bigr).

Therefore,

\displaystyle|f^{*}(x)-\overline{f}(x)|\leq|f^{*}(x)-f^{*}(y_{x})|+|f^{*}(y_{x})-h(Ax)|.

First term.

By the
(p,C)
-smoothness of
f^{*}
,

\displaystyle|f^{*}(x)-f^{*}(y_{x})|\leq C\,\|x-y_{x}\|^{p\wedge 1}\leq C\,\tau^{p\wedge 1}.

Second term.

Since
y_{x}\in\mathcal{M}
, we have
Ay_{x}\in A(\mathcal{M})
. By definition of

\widetilde{f}
and the inverse map
T
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}(Ay_{x})=f^{*}(T(Ay_{x}))=f^{*}(y_{x}).

Since
\widetilde{F}=\widetilde{f}
on
A(\mathcal{M})
, it follows that

\displaystyle f^{*}(y_{x})=\widetilde{F}(Ay_{x}).

Hence

\displaystyle|f^{*}(y_{x})-h(Ax)|=|\widetilde{F}(Ay_{x})-h(Ax)|\leq|\widetilde{F}(Ay_{x})-\widetilde{F}(Ax)|+|\widetilde{F}(Ax)-h(Ax)|.

The second term is bounded by

\displaystyle|\widetilde{F}(Ax)-h(Ax)|\leq\|\widetilde{F}-h\|_{\infty,E}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta},

since
x\in\mathcal{M}_{\tau}\subset[0,1]^{d}
implies
Ax\in A(\mathcal{M}_{\tau})\subset E
.

For the first term, since
AA^{\top}=\frac{d}{d_{\delta}}I_{d_{\delta}}
in
Step 1
, we have

\displaystyle\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sqrt{\frac{d}{d_{\delta}}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|Ay_{x}-Ax\|\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|y_{x}-x\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{d}{d_{\delta}}}\,\tau.

Using that
\widetilde{F}
is
(p,C_{\mathrm{ext}}\,C(1-\delta)^{-p})
-smooth on
E
,
we obtain

\displaystyle|\widetilde{F}(Ay_{x})-\widetilde{F}(Ax)|\leq C_{\mathrm{ext}}\,C(1-\delta)^{-p}\|Ay_{x}-Ax\|^{p\wedge 1}\leq C_{\mathrm{ext}}\,C(1-\delta)^{-p}\bigl(\sqrt{\frac{d}{d_{\delta}}}\,\tau\bigr)^{p\wedge 1}.

Combining the preceding bounds yields

\displaystyle|f^{*}(x)-\overline{f}(x)|\leq C\,\tau^{p\wedge 1}+C_{\mathrm{ext}}\,C(1-\delta)^{-p}\bigl(\sqrt{\frac{d}{d_{\delta}}}\,\tau\bigr)^{p\wedge 1}+\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Define

\displaystyle C_{1}:=C+C_{\mathrm{ext}}\,C(1-\delta)^{-p}\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{d}{d_{\delta}}}\right)^{p\wedge 1}.

Then, taking the supremum over
x\in\mathcal{M}_{\tau}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}_{\rho}}\leq C_{1}\,\tau^{p\wedge 1}+C_{2}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta},

for some positive constant
C_{2}=C_{p,C_{\mathrm{ext}},d,d_{\delta},\delta}
.
Under condition (
95
),

\displaystyle\tau\leq c_{p,C,d,d_{M},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{1/(p\wedge 1)},

we obtain

\displaystyle C_{1}\tau^{p\wedge 1}\leq C_{1}c_{p,C,d,d_{M},\delta}^{\,p\wedge 1}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Choosing
c_{p,C,d,d_{M},\delta}
sufficiently small (depending only on

C_{1}
), we ensure that

\displaystyle C_{1}\tau^{p\wedge 1}\leq C_{3}\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}_{\tau}}\leq(C_{2}+C_{3})\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Squaring both sides yields (
96
).
∎

Theorem 38

(Temporal-spatial risk bound under approximate manifold support)
.

Suppose that Assumptions 1, 2, 3
a
–
b
, and 4 hold. Assume moreover that the design points are supported on
\mathcal{M}_{\tau}
, where
\mathcal{M}\subset[0,1]^{d}
is a compact
d_{M}
-dimensional manifold, and that
f^{*}
satisfies the smoothness conditions of Lemma
37
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
, let
d_{\delta}
be as in Lemma
37
, and assume (
95
). Let
\widehat{f}
be the estimator in Equation (2), with
(L,r)
chosen as in Lemma
37
, and let

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}\asymp\max\{\sigma_{\epsilon},\sigma_{\gamma}\}\sqrt{\log(nm)}

and
(a_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be any sequence such that
a_{n}\to\infty
.
Then, let
(S_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be any sequence satisfying

\displaystyle C_{\beta}\log(n)\leq S_{n}\leq n,

for a sufficiently large constant
C_{\beta}>0
depending only on the mixing rate in Assumption 4.
Suppose that the entropy conditions of Theorem
35
hold with sequences
(\rho_{n})
and
(\widetilde{\rho}_{n})
. Then, with probability approaching one,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\,\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(97)

Using the canonical choice
(L,r)
from Lemma
37
together with Lemma
24
, we may take

\displaystyle\rho_{n}\lesssim\bigl(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{4.3}(nm),\qquad\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\bigl(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)

(98)

\displaystyle+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(99)

Moreover choosing
S_{n}\asymp\log n
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}+1)(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{7.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\frac{(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm)}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

(100)

with probability approaching one.

Proof.

The proof follows the same line of argument as the proof of Theorem
35
, with the approximation input supplied by Lemma
37
. The only additional point is that the approximation bound from Lemma
37
is available only on the support of the design, namely
\mathcal{M}_{\tau}
. Accordingly, we work with
\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
throughout.

By Lemma
37
, there exists
\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
such that

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}_{\tau}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2},\qquad\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}:=(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}.

Since the support of the design is contained in
\mathcal{M}_{\tau}
, it follows that for every observed design point
x_{ij}
,

\displaystyle|f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}(x_{ij})|^{2}\leq\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}_{\tau}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}.

Moreover, since
\|f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{A}_{nm}/4
by Assumption 3
b
, and
\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}\to 0
, for all sufficiently large
nm
,

\displaystyle|\overline{f}(x_{ij})|\leq|f^{*}(x_{ij})|+|f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}(x_{ij})|\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}+\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{2}.

Hence, for all sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x_{ij})=\overline{f}(x_{ij})\qquad\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n,\ 1\leq j\leq m_{i}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}.

(101)

Define

\displaystyle\Delta_{nm}(x):=\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x)-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x),

and the shifted truncated class

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f}):=\bigl\{f_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}:\,f\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)\bigr\}.

For every
0<\delta\leq 1
, every
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r)
, and every empirical norm appearing in the proof of Theorem
35
, translation by
-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
is an isometry. Therefore the blockwise entropy condition and the global entropy condition carry over to the shifted class
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
with the same sequences
(\rho_{n})
and
(\widetilde{\rho}_{n})
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=\bigl\{|\epsilon_{ij}|\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\,\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n,\,1\leq j\leq m_{i},\quad\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\infty,nm}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\,\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n\bigr\}.

By Lemma
2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})=o(1).

Using

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}=\Delta_{nm}+(\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},

(102)

where we used (
101
) together with the fact that the approximation error is controlled on the support of the design.

It therefore suffices to control
\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}
.

On the event
\mathcal{E}
, exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
, the empirical basic inequality gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x_{ij})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})+\epsilon_{ij}\bigr\}.

(103)

We first control the approximation term. By Cauchy–Schwarz and (
101
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x_{ij})\bigr\}\leq\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\,\|f^{*}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\|_{nm}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}.

(104)

Step 1: the
\epsilon
-term.

Since
\Delta_{nm}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, the blockwise entropy condition implies that the proof of

Corollary

19

can be repeated block by block for the shifted class
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, with the quantity
\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{\cdot,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)
replaced by
\rho_{n}
. Thus, arguing exactly as in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
35
, we obtain that with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq C\,\sigma_{\epsilon}\,\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}(\Delta_{nm}),

(105)

where
\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}
is defined exactly as
\Lambda_{n,S}
in

Corollary

19
, but with
\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{\cdot,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)
replaced by
\rho_{n}
. Repeating the same Jensen reduction as in the proof of Theorem
35
gives

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}(\Delta_{nm})\leq C\,\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\,\log\!\left(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\right)}.

(106)

Applying Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr).

(107)

Since
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}
, this becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr).

(108)

Step 2: norm comparison.

Using again the blockwise entropy condition, the proofs of

Corollary

10

and

Lemma

20

carry over block by block with
\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{5.3}(nm/S_{n})
replaced by
\rho_{n}\log(nm)
. Therefore, for any sequence
a_{n}\to\infty
, arguing exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
, we obtain with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+Ca_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}.

(109)

Step 3: the
\gamma
-term.

We decompose the
\gamma
-term exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})

\displaystyle={}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}.

For the second term, by Lemma
29
and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}.

(110)

Combining this with (
109
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\leq\frac{8\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{1}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}.

(111)

For the centered fluctuation term, we now use the global entropy condition. Since
\Delta_{nm}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, the proof of

Corollary

13

carries over verbatim with
\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)
replaced by
\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)
. Hence, with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C\,\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\,\log(nm).

(112)

Using (
109
) once more, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{2}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}+C_{3}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm).

(113)

Combining (
111
) and
(
113
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\leq\frac{3}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{4}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}+C_{5}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+C_{6}\,\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(114)

Step 4: empirical norm bound.

Substituting (
104
),
(
108
), and
(
114
) into
(
103
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(115)

We now control the logarithmic factor exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
. If

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n},

then (
115
) already yields the desired conclusion. Otherwise,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}>\frac{1}{n},\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\geq n^{-1/2},

so that

\displaystyle\log\!\left(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\right)\leq\log\!\left(2\mathcal{A}_{nm}\sqrt{n}\right)\lesssim\log(nm).

Therefore, in this case,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(116)

Since
a_{n}\to\infty
, we have
a_{n}\geq 1
for all sufficiently large
n
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)\leq a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm).

Hence in either case,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(117)

Step 5: passage to
\mathcal{L}_{2}
.

Combining (
109
) and
(
117
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+Ca_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(118)

Substituting (
118
) into
(
102
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\,\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

(119)

with probability tending to one.

It remains to bound
\rho_{n}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{n}
. Under the canonical choice of
(L,r)
from Lemma
37
, Lemma
24
yields

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm})\lesssim nm\,(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1}),

and therefore

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Likewise, for the blockwise empirical norms, since
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m\asymp nm/S_{n}
and
|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m\asymp nm/S_{n}
, the same entropy bound gives

\displaystyle\rho_{n}\lesssim\left(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Substituting these bounds into (
119
) yields

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\bigl(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

Finally, choosing
S_{n}\asymp\log n
, and using

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\asymp(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm),\qquad\beta(S_{n})\lesssim\frac{1}{n},

we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}+a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\bigl(\frac{\log n}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{6.3}(nm)

\displaystyle+a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{6.3}(nm)+a_{n}\frac{(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm)}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

Since

\displaystyle(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\leq(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{7.3}(nm),

and

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\log n}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{6.3}(nm)\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{7.3}(nm),

it follows that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}+1)(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{\delta}}}\log^{7.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\frac{(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm)}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

(120)

with probability tending to one.
This completes the proof.
∎

S13
Low Minkowski Dimension Support under Temporal-Spatial Dependence

In this section, we extend the temporal-spatial risk bound to the case where the design is supported on a set with low Minkowski dimension. The stochastic part of the argument is unchanged from the previous sections; the only new ingredient is the approximation lemma below.

The proof follows the strategy of Theorem 6.3 in
Jiao et al. (2023)
and, more specifically, Appendix A.9 of the supplementary material. In that proof, the support of the design is projected into a lower-dimensional space using a generalized Johnson–Lindenstrauss lemma for infinite sets, the target function is represented on the projected set, then extended by Whitney’s extension theorem and approximated there by ReLU networks. See Section 6.3 of the main paper and Appendix A.9 of the supplement.

We start defining Minkowski dimension.

Definition 2

(Minkowski dimension)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be bounded. For
\epsilon>0
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{M},\|\cdot\|)

denote the covering number of
\mathcal{M}
by Euclidean balls of radius
\epsilon
.
The upper and lower Minkowski dimensions of
\mathcal{M}
are defined by

\displaystyle\overline{\dim}_{M}(\mathcal{M}):=\limsup_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{A},\|\cdot\|)}{\log(1/\epsilon)},\qquad\underline{\dim}_{M}(\mathcal{M}):=\liminf_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{A},\|\cdot\|)}{\log(1/\epsilon)}.

If these two quantities coincide, their common value is called the Minkowski dimension of
\mathcal{M}
, denoted by
\dim_{M}(\mathcal{M})
.

Lemma 39

(Approximation under low Minkowski dimension support)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\subset[0,1]^{d}
be a compact set with Minkowski dimension

\displaystyle d^{*}:=\dim_{M}(\mathcal{M})<d.

Assume that
f^{*}:[0,1]^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is
(p,C)
-smooth, with
p=q+s
,
q\in\mathbf{N}
,
s\in(0,1]
, and that all partial derivatives of order at most
q
are uniformly bounded.

Fix
\delta,\tau\in(0,1)
. Let
d_{0}\in\mathbf{Z}^{+}
satisfy

\displaystyle d_{0}\geq\kappa\frac{c\,d^{*}+\log(2/\tau)}{\delta^{2}},

where
\kappa,c>0
are constants from the generalized Johnson–Lindenstrauss lemma in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
. Define

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}:=(nm)^{-\frac{p}{2p+d_{0}}}.

Then there exists
\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
, with either

\displaystyle L\asymp\log(nm),\qquad r\asymp(nm)^{\frac{d_{0}}{2(2p+d_{0})}}\log(nm),

or

\displaystyle L\asymp\log(nm)\,(nm)^{\frac{d_{0}}{2(2p+d_{0})}},\qquad r=O(1),

such that

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}^{2}\leq C_{p,C,d,d^{*},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2},

(121)

where

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}:=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{M}}|f(x)|.

The constant
C_{p,C,d,d^{*},\delta}
does not depend on
n
or
m
.

Proof.

The proof follows Appendix A.9 of the supplementary material of
Jiao et al. (2023)
.

Step 1: Dimension reduction via the generalized Johnson–Lindenstrauss argument.

Let
E:=\mathcal{M}\subset[0,1]^{d}
. Since
\mathcal{M}
is compact, we may work directly with

E
in place of the closure
\bar{E}
used in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
. Define

\displaystyle\bar{T}:=\{x-y:x,y\in E\}.

For any
\delta>0
, define

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{\delta}:=\{\bar{t}\in\bar{T}:\|\bar{t}\|_{2}\geq\delta\},\qquad T_{\delta}:=\left\{\frac{\bar{t}}{\|\bar{t}\|_{2}}:\bar{t}\in\bar{T}_{\delta}\right\},

and

\displaystyle T:=\left\{\frac{\bar{t}}{\|\bar{t}\|_{2}}:\bar{t}\in\bar{T}\setminus\{0\}\right\}.

As shown in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
, one first has

\displaystyle\dim_{M}(\bar{T})\leq 2d^{*}.

Moreover, for every
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\dim_{M}\!\bigl(\delta^{-1}\bar{T}_{\delta}\bigr)\leq 4d^{*},\qquad\dim_{M}(T_{\delta})\leq\dim_{M}\!\bigl(\delta^{-1}\bar{T}_{\delta}\bigr)\leq 4d^{*}.

Indeed, Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
shows that

\displaystyle\dim_{M}(T_{\delta})\leq\dim_{M}\!\bigl(\delta^{-1}\bar{T}_{\delta}\bigr)\leq 2d^{*}+\dim_{M}(\bar{T}_{\delta})\leq 2d^{*}+\dim_{M}(\bar{T})\leq 4d^{*}.

Using the corresponding covering-number comparison between
T_{\delta}
and

\delta^{-1}\bar{T}_{\delta}
, Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
then yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{H(\epsilon,\|\cdot\|_{2},T)}\leq c_{1}\sqrt{d^{*}\log(1/\epsilon)},

for some constant
c_{1}>0
, where

\displaystyle H(\epsilon,\|\cdot\|_{2},T):=\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,T,\|\cdot\|_{2}).

They then define

\displaystyle\gamma(T):=\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{H(\epsilon,\|\cdot\|_{2},T)}\,d\epsilon,

and conclude that

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}(T)=c\,d^{*}

for some constant
c>0
. Therefore, if

\displaystyle d_{0}\geq\kappa\frac{\gamma^{2}(T)+\log(2/\tau)}{\delta^{2}}=\kappa\frac{c\,d^{*}+\log(2/\tau)}{\delta^{2}},

where
\kappa>0
is the universal constant appearing in the generalized Johnson–Lindenstrauss lemma and
\tau\in(0,1)
is its confidence parameter, then the generalized Johnson–Lindenstrauss lemma used in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
yields a linear map

\displaystyle A:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{0}}

whose restriction to
\bar{E}
is injective and such that

\displaystyle(1-\delta)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|Ax_{1}-Ax_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\leq(1+\delta)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|_{2}^{2},\qquad x_{1},x_{2}\in\bar{E}.

Moreover, as in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
, we have

\displaystyle A(E)\subset A([0,1]^{d})\subset\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}:=\left[-\sqrt{d/d_{0}},\,\sqrt{d/d_{0}}\right]^{d_{0}}.

Step 2: Low-dimensional representation on the projected set.

Since
A
is injective on
\bar{E}
, for each
z\in A(\bar{E})
there exists a unique

x_{z}\in\bar{E}
such that
Ax_{z}=z
. Define the selection map

\displaystyle SL:A(\bar{E})\to\bar{E},\qquad SL(z):=x_{z}.

By the same argument as in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
, the map
SL
is
differentiable and its derivative satisfies

\displaystyle\|DSL(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\in\left[\sqrt{\frac{1}{1+\delta}},\,\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-\delta}}\right],\qquad z\in A(\bar{E}).

Define the function

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}(z):=f^{*}(x_{z})=f^{*}(SL(z)),\qquad z\in A(\bar{E}).

Since
f^{*}
is
(p,C)
-smooth on
[0,1]^{d}
, and the selection map
SL

is differentiable with

\displaystyle\|DSL(z)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1-\delta)^{-1/2},\qquad z\in A(\bar{E}),

it follows, as in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
, that

\widetilde{f}
is
(p,\,C(1-\delta)^{-p/2})
-smooth on
A(\bar{E})

in the sense of Definition 2 in the Main Manuscript.

Step 3: Whitney extension on the projected box.

Since
\bar{E}
is compact and
A
is linear, the set
A(\bar{E})\subset\mathbb{R}^{d_{0}}
is compact,
hence closed. Moreover,
A(\bar{E})\subset\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}
. Therefore, by the extended version of Whitney’s extension theorem in
Fefferman (2006)
, there exists a function

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}:\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}\to\mathbb{R}

such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}(z)=\widetilde{f}(z),\qquad z\in A(\bar{E}),

and
\widetilde{F}
is
(p,C_{\mathrm{ext}}\,C(1-\delta)^{-p/2})
-smooth on
\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}
, in the sense of Definition 2 in the Main Manuscript, where
C_{\mathrm{ext}}>0
depends only on
p
and
d_{0}
.

Step 4: Approximation in dimension
d_{0}
.

We now approximate
\widetilde{F}
on the box
\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d_{0}}
. After an affine rescaling mapping
\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}
onto
[0,1]^{d_{0}}
, Corollary 3.1 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
applies in dimension
d_{0}
. Therefore, there exists a ReLU network

\displaystyle h:\mathbb{R}^{d_{0}}\to\mathbb{R}

with either one of the two stated architectures such that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{F}-h\|_{\infty,\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}}\leq C_{p,C,C_{\mathrm{ext}},d,d_{0},\delta}\,(nm)^{-\frac{p}{2p+d_{0}}}=C_{p,C,C_{\mathrm{ext}},d,d_{0},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Here the constant
C_{p,C,C_{\mathrm{ext}},d,d_{0},\delta}
absorbs the factor

(1-\delta)^{-p/2}
coming from the Hölder norm of
\widetilde{F}
.
This is the same low-dimensional approximation step used after constructing
\widetilde{F}_{0}
in Appendix A.9 of
Jiao et al. (2023)
.

Step 5: Lifting back to the ambient space.

Define

\displaystyle\overline{f}(x):=h(Ax),\qquad x\in[0,1]^{d}.

Since
A
is linear, the composition
x\mapsto Ax
can be absorbed into the first
layer of the network. Hence
\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
with the same order
of depth and width as
h
.

In fact, if
h\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
, then by Definition 1 in the Main Manuscript,

\displaystyle h(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{1,i}^{(L)}f_{i}^{(L)}(z)+c_{1,0}^{(L)},

where the functions
f_{i}^{(s)}
satisfy the recursive relations in Equations (4)–(5) of the Main Manuscript. For the first layer,

\displaystyle f_{i}^{(1)}(Ax)

\displaystyle=\sigma\!\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{0}}c_{i,j}^{(0)}(Ax)^{(j)}+c_{i,0}^{(0)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{0}}c_{i,j}^{(0)}A_{j,k}\right)x^{(k)}+c_{i,0}^{(0)}\right),

where
\sigma
denotes the ReLU activation function. Thus, after redefining the first-layer coefficients by

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}_{i,k}^{(0)}:=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{0}}c_{i,j}^{(0)}A_{j,k},

the composition
x\mapsto h(Ax)
has the same recursive form as Equations (4)–(5) in the Main Manuscript, with the same number of layers
L
and the same width
r
. Hence

\displaystyle\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r).

Now let
x\in E=\mathcal{M}
. Since
x\in\bar{E}
, we have
Ax\in A(\bar{E})
, and therefore

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}(Ax)=\widetilde{f}(Ax)=f^{*}(x).

Hence

\displaystyle|f^{*}(x)-\overline{f}(x)|=|\widetilde{F}(Ax)-h(Ax)|\leq\|\widetilde{F}-h\|_{\infty,\mathcal{B}_{d_{0}}}\leq C_{p,C,C_{\mathrm{ext}},d,d_{0},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Taking the supremum over
x\in\mathcal{M}
yields

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}\leq C_{p,C,C_{\mathrm{ext}},d,d_{0},\delta}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}.

Squaring both sides gives (
121
).
∎

Theorem 40

(Temporal-spatial risk bound under low Minkowski dimension support)
.

Suppose that Assumptions 1, 2, 3
a
–
b
, and 4 hold. Assume moreover that the design points are supported on a set
\mathcal{M}\subset[0,1]^{d}
with Minkowski dimension

\displaystyle d^{*}:=\dim_{M}(\mathcal{M})<d,

and that
f^{*}
satisfies the smoothness conditions of Lemma
39
.

Fix
\delta,\tau\in(0,1)
, let
d_{0}
be as in Lemma
39
, and let
\widehat{f}
be the estimator in Equation (2), with
(L,r)
chosen as in Lemma
39
. Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}\asymp\max\{\sigma_{\epsilon},\sigma_{\gamma}\}\sqrt{\log(nm)}

and let
(a_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be any sequence such that
a_{n}\to\infty
. Let
(S_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be any sequence satisfying

\displaystyle C_{\beta}\log(n)\leq S_{n}\leq n,

for a sufficiently large constant
C_{\beta}>0
depending only on the mixing rate in Assumption 4.

Suppose that the blockwise and global entropy conditions of Theorem
35
hold with sequences
(\rho_{n})
and
(\widetilde{\rho}_{n})
. Then, with probability approaching one,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\,\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(122)

In particular, using
(L,r)
chosen as in Lemma
39
, we may take

\displaystyle\rho_{n}\lesssim\bigl(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{4.3}(nm),\qquad\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\bigl(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

(123)

with probability approaching one.

Moreover, choosing
S_{n}\asymp\log(n)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}+1)(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{7.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\frac{(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm)}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

(124)

with probability approaching one.

Proof.

The proof follows the same line of argument as the proof of Theorem
35
, with the approximation input supplied by Lemma
39
. The only additional point is that the approximation bound from Lemma
39
is available only on the support of the design, namely
\mathcal{M}
. Accordingly, we work with
\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
throughout.

By Lemma
39
, there exists
\overline{f}\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)
such that

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2},\qquad\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}:=(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}.

Since the support of the design is contained in
\mathcal{M}
, it follows that for every observed design point
x_{ij}
,

\displaystyle|f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}(x_{ij})|^{2}\leq\|f^{*}-\overline{f}\|_{\infty,\mathcal{M}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}.

Moreover, since
\|f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{A}_{nm}/4
by Assumption 3
b
, and
\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}\to 0
, for all sufficiently large
nm
,

\displaystyle|\overline{f}(x_{ij})|\leq|f^{*}(x_{ij})|+|f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}(x_{ij})|\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}+\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{2}.

Hence, for all sufficiently large
nm
,

\displaystyle\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x_{ij})=\overline{f}(x_{ij})\qquad\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n,\ 1\leq j\leq m_{i}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\|f^{*}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}.

(125)

Define

\displaystyle\Delta_{nm}(x):=\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x)-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x),

and the shifted truncated class

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f}):=\bigl\{f_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}:\,f\in\mathcal{F}(L,r)\bigr\}.

For every
0<\delta\leq 1
, every
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r)
, and every empirical norm appearing in the proof of Theorem
35
, translation by
-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}
is an isometry. Therefore the blockwise entropy condition and the global entropy condition carry over to the shifted class
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
with the same sequences
(\rho_{n})
and
(\widetilde{\rho}_{n})
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=\bigl\{|\epsilon_{ij}|\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\,\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n,\,1\leq j\leq m_{i},\quad\|\gamma_{i}\|_{\infty,nm}\leq\frac{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{4}\,\text{for all }1\leq i\leq n\bigr\}.

By Lemma
2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})=o(1).

Using

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}=\Delta_{nm}+(\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2},

(126)

where we used (
125
) together with the fact that the approximation error is controlled on the support of the design.

It therefore suffices to control
\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}
.

On the event
\mathcal{E}
, exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
, the empirical basic inequality gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x_{ij})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})+\epsilon_{ij}\bigr\}.

(127)

We first control the approximation term. By Cauchy–Schwarz and (
125
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\bigl\{f^{*}(x_{ij})-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(x_{ij})\bigr\}\leq\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\,\|f^{*}-\overline{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}\|_{nm}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}.

(128)

Step 1: the
\epsilon
-term.

Since
\Delta_{nm}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, the blockwise entropy condition implies that the proof of

Corollary

19

can be repeated block by block for the shifted class
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, with the quantity
\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{\cdot,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)
replaced by
\rho_{n}
. Thus, arguing exactly as in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
35
, we obtain that with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq C\,\sigma_{\epsilon}\,\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}(\Delta_{nm}),

(129)

where
\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}
is defined exactly as
\Lambda_{n,S}
in

Corollary

19
, but with
\phi_{|\mathcal{J}_{\cdot,s}|m}\log^{4.3}(nm)
replaced by
\rho_{n}
. Repeating the same Jensen reduction as in the proof of Theorem
35
gives

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(\rho)}_{n,S_{n}}(\Delta_{nm})\leq C\,\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\,\log\!\left(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\right)}.

(130)

Applying Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr).

(131)

Since
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}
, this becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\epsilon_{ij}\leq\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C\,\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr).

(132)

Step 2: norm comparison.

Using again the blockwise entropy condition, the proofs of

Corollary

10

and

Lemma

20

carry over block by block with
\phi_{nm/S_{n}}\log^{5.3}(nm/S_{n})
replaced by
\rho_{n}\log(nm)
. Therefore, for any sequence
a_{n}\to\infty
, arguing exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
, we obtain with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+Ca_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}.

(133)

Step 3: the
\gamma
-term.

We decompose the
\gamma
-term exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})

\displaystyle={}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}.

For the second term, by Lemma
29
and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{i}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{32}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}.

(134)

Combining this with (
133
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\leq\frac{8\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}+\frac{1}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{1}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}.

(135)

For the centered fluctuation term, we now use the global entropy condition. Since
\Delta_{nm}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(\overline{f})
, the proof of

Corollary

13

carries over verbatim with
\phi_{nm}\log^{5.3}(nm)
replaced by
\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)
. Hence, with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}+C\,\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\,\log(nm).

(136)

Using (
133
) once more, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Bigl\{\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})-\langle\gamma_{i},\Delta_{nm}\rangle_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+C_{2}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}+C_{3}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm).

(137)

Combining (
135
) and
(
137
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\frac{1}{nm_{i}}\Delta_{nm}(x_{ij})\gamma_{i}(x_{ij})\leq\frac{3}{16}\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{4}a_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}+C_{5}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+C_{6}\,\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(138)

Step 4: empirical norm bound.

Substituting (
128
),
(
132
), and
(
138
) into
(
127
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\bigr)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(139)

We now control the logarithmic factor exactly as in the proof of Theorem
35
. If

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n},

then (
139
) already yields the desired conclusion. Otherwise,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}>\frac{1}{n},\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}\geq n^{-1/2},

so that

\displaystyle\log\!\left(\frac{2\mathcal{A}_{nm}}{\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}}\right)\leq\log\!\left(2\mathcal{A}_{nm}\sqrt{n}\right)\lesssim\log(nm).

Therefore, in this case,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(140)

Since
a_{n}\to\infty
, we have
a_{n}\geq 1
for all sufficiently large
n
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)\leq a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm).

Hence in either case,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(141)

Step 5: passage to
\mathcal{L}_{2}
.

Combining (
133
) and
(
141
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\|\Delta_{nm}\|_{nm}^{2}+Ca_{n}\bigl\{\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{nm,\delta}^{2}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\rho_{n}\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\log(nm)+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n}.

(142)

Substituting (
142
) into
(
126
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\rho_{n}\,\log(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\,\log(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

(143)

with probability tending to one. This proves (
122
).

It remains to derive (
123
) and (
124
). Under the canonical choices of
(L,r)
from Lemma
39
, Lemma
24
yields the global entropy bound

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}\!\bigl(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}(L,r),\|\cdot\|_{nm}\bigr)\lesssim nm\,(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{4.3}(nm)\log(\delta^{-1}),

and therefore

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}_{n}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Likewise, for the blockwise empirical norms, since
|\mathcal{J}_{e,s}|m\asymp nm/S_{n}
and
|\mathcal{J}_{o,s}|m\asymp nm/S_{n}
, the same entropy bound gives

\displaystyle\rho_{n}\lesssim\left(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{4.3}(nm).

Substituting these bounds into (
143
) yields

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\bigl(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)

\displaystyle+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)+a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

which proves (
123
).

Now choose
S_{n}\asymp\log n
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\right)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\lesssim(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log(nm),

since
S_{n}\asymp\log n
and
\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}\leq 1
. Also, by the choice

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\asymp(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm),

we obtain

\displaystyle a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\bigl(\frac{S_{n}}{nm}\bigr)^{\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)\lesssim a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{7.3}(nm).

Moreover,

\displaystyle a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{5.3}(nm)\lesssim a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{6.3}(nm),

so this term is dominated by the previous one. Finally, by the exponential
\beta
-mixing assumption and
S_{n}\asymp\log n
,

\displaystyle\beta(S_{n})\lesssim\frac{1}{n},

and therefore

\displaystyle a_{n}\mathcal{A}_{nm}^{2}\beta(S_{n})\lesssim a_{n}\frac{(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm)}{n}.

Substituting these bounds into the previous display yields

\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}_{\mathcal{A}_{nm}}-f^{*}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;a_{n}(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}+1)(nm)^{-\frac{2p}{2p+d_{0}}}\log^{7.3}(nm)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{n}\frac{(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}+\sigma_{\gamma}^{2})\log(nm)}{n}+\frac{\sigma_{\gamma}^{2}}{n},

(144)

with probability tending to one, which is exactly (
124
).
∎
